\mnote{Identity~\eqref{mrn:s:tv:eq:glue} is the vertex-sum identity of source~74 (display~\eqref{mrn:w:rf:eq:vertices}) and of \prp's Part~VI; source~74's star transfer (Section~\ref{mrn:w:rf:sec:stars}) treats $2+q$ covers in the same way.}
Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:tv:thm:marginal}, with the other two left variables specialized to
$t u_i$ and the root variable to $x$, gives
\[
 K(s,t,x)=C_h(s,t)+xD_h(s,t),\qquad
 C_h=s^3C(t/s),\quad D_h=s^2D(t/s).
\]
The product $FK$ is Lorentzian \cite[Corollary 2.32]{b:BH}. Retain only terms
of $x$-degree at most one, then set $x=u_vt$. The result is
\[
 A_hC_h+u_vt(B_hC_h+A_hD_h)
       =s^{q+3}P_G(t/s),
\]
which is Lorentzian. The case $q=0$ is Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:tv:thm:marginal} itself.
\end{proof}

For completeness, the partial truncation used here preserves the Lorentzian
property directly. Its support is the intersection of an $M$-convex set with
the coordinate bound $\deg_x\le1$, which is again $M$-convex when nonempty.
In a quadratic coordinate derivative, a differentiation using no $x$ simply
removes the nonnegative $x^2$ coefficient from the original quadratic; this
subtracts a positive semidefinite diagonal Hessian entry and cannot increase
the positive index. A derivative using $x$ once is the corresponding derivative
of $\partial_x(FK)$ specialized at $x=0$. A derivative using $x$ twice or more
vanishes. The Hessian criterion gives the assertion.

\begin{corollary}\label{mrn:s:tv:cor:starsix}
Every genuine $3+3$-cover graph whose core has matching rank at most one has
$P_G\in\ULC_6$ for arbitrary positive activities on both shores.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
A nonempty bipartite graph of matching rank one is a star. A row star is
Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:tv:thm:star}; a column star follows after interchanging the shores.
For an empty core, the polynomial is a product of two smaller-shore
$\ULC_3$ polynomials, so product closure applies.
\end{proof}
The proof also retains every non-root left activity as its own variable:
the selected-left marginal of the row-star cover, homogenized to degree
$q+3$, is Lorentzian. The scalar conclusion then allows arbitrary left
activities. This vertex-sum result does not provide a rank-three exterior-block
lift for general cores.

\subsection{Exact verification and scope}\label{mrn:s:tv:sec:verification}
The source package contains the manuscript, the complete determinant
coefficient list and three standard-library Python checks. \mnote{Shipped as \path{code/10-three-vertex-*.py} and \path{data/10-three-vertex-*.json}; the determinant list is \path{data/10-three-vertex-determinant.json}.} From its root run
\[
 \texttt{python -O code/replay\_all.py}.
\]
The two independent polynomial reconstructions produce the same lexicographically
ordered coefficient list. Its canonical SHA-256 is
\begin{center}\small
\texttt{0f6b0733bd999e34e058223139f524830}\\
\texttt{bcdcd6a1436a8fae89983cba49c3522}.
\end{center}
The third checker independently enumerates endpoint sets in $240$ weighted
three-left graphs and $160$ weighted star-core graphs, including $38$ genuine
minimum covers. It verifies the overlap identity, Rayleigh specialization,
rooted gluing and all fixed-cover Newton inequalities. These finite regressions
supplement the proof; only the complete determinant expansion is used as a
finite certificate for a universal assertion.

The result concerns all fields in the stated star-core class. The general
finite-activity one-shore rank-six problem remains open at its three middle
Newton gaps. \mnote{After source~05 only two middle gaps remain: at matching number six with one weighted shore, gap one holds by \prp's Theorem~180.2, gap two by source~05 (Section~\ref{mrn:src:05}) and gap five by source~13 (Section~\ref{mrn:src:13}), at every activity; gaps three and four are open in general.} No universal real-rootedness or arbitrary-core Lorentzian
recombination is claimed. The standard Lorentzian and Rayleigh theorems are
attributed below; no exhaustive priority claim is made.

%% ======================== source 23 ========================
\section{Rooted Lorentzian Hall sectors and an unrooted cubic obstruction (source~23)}\label{mrn:src:23}
\paragraph{Abstract of source~23, as delivered.} For a genuine $a+q$ bipartite cover, condition on all $q$ right-core vertices and on any matchable $q-2$ exterior-left vertices. The remaining degree-$a$ exterior-right marginal is Lorentzian, for arbitrary positive activities on both shores. This follows from exact weighted Hall-slack compression and the rank-two exterior-block matroid lift. The roots cannot simply be omitted: a connected 25-vertex rank-six graph, with unit left activities and a unique $3+3$ minimum cover, has an all-right-core conditional cubic failing both degree-three Newton inequalities. Its shifted degree-six inequalities remain strict, and its full polynomial is strictly $\ULC_6$ for every common right-core activity. This identifies a normalization and recombination barrier without refuting rank-six ULC.

\subsection{Matching supports and the rooted theorem}
For a bipartite graph with left and right activities $u_x,v_y>0$, its matching-support polynomial is
\[
 P_G(t)=\sum_{\substack{|I|=|J|\\G[I,J]\text{ has a perfect matching}}}
                 u_Iv_Jt^{|I|}.
\]
Each feasible endpoint pair is counted once. Call a displayed cover $A\sqcup B$ genuine when
\[
 X=A\sqcup L,\quad Y=B\sqcup R,\quad
 |A|=a,\quad |B|=q,\quad \nu(G)=a+q.
\]
There are no $L$--$R$ edges. Every maximum matching consists of disjoint exterior matchings of sizes $a$ and $q$.

\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:s:rs:thm:rooted}
Let $a\ge1$, $q\ge2$, and let $F\subseteq L$ be any matchable set of $q-2$ vertices. Condition simultaneously on selecting all $B$ and all $F$. After removing the size factor $t^q$ and fixed positive activity factors, the remaining polynomial has degree $a$ and is $\ULC_a$.

More strongly, keep the exterior-right activities as separate variables. The degree-$a$ homogenization
\[
 H_F(s,z_R)=\sum_{J\subseteq R}c_F(J)z_Js^{a-|J|}
\]
is Lorentzian, where $c_F(J)$ sums the fixed left activities of feasible endpoint sets containing $F$ and matched to $B\cup J$.
\end{theorem}

The theorem permits arbitrary positive activities on both shores. The fixed positive normalization does not affect the Lorentzian or Newton properties. In particular, in a genuine $3+3$ cover, forcing all three $B$ vertices and one specified exterior-left vertex leaves a degree-three Lorentzian marginal.

\subsubsection{Exact inputs}
We use the following previously proved weighted results \cite{b:m60} \mnote{source~60: Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:rb:thm:matrix}, Proposition~\ref{mrn:s:rb:prop:smallslack} and Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:rb:thm:kernel}; identity~\eqref{mrn:s:rs:eq:kernelweights} is source~60's~\eqref{mrn:s:rb:eq:pairweights}. They are restated here as source~23 states them}; the compression is a regular-minor construction and need not produce an ordinary graph.

First, if a matrix consists of $a$ ordinary columns and an exterior column block of rank two, with total rank $a+2$, its degree-$(a+2)$ left marginal of nonzero square minors is Lorentzian after fixing positive column activities.

Second, a matchable forced set of size $h$ with $h$, $h+1$ or $h+2$ neighbors admits exact weighted compression. Slack zero deletes the forced set and its neighbors. Slack one replaces the neighbors by a weighted union-profile element. Slack two replaces them by a two-dimensional kernel-line family. The compressed matrix has rank reduced by $h$, and its support polynomial recovers the conditioned polynomial after a positive factor and $t^h$.

For precision, in the slack-two case let $\Gamma$ be the forced-column matrix on its $h+2$ neighboring rows, write $\alpha_i$ for their activities, and put
\[
 E_h=\sum_{\substack{|I|=h\\\det\Gamma[I]\ne0}}\alpha_I.
\]
Each eligible omitted index $i$ gives a kernel line in
$\ker(\Gamma^{\mathsf T})\cap\{x_i=0\}$, with virtual activity
$\alpha_{N\setminus\{i\}}/E_h$. Independent kernel pairs have a common residual determinant profile, and their total activity is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:rs:eq:kernelweights}
 \sum_{\text{independent kernel pairs }i<j}
 \frac{\alpha_{N\setminus\{i\}}\alpha_{N\setminus\{j\}}}{E_h^2}
 =\frac{\alpha_N}{E_h}.
\end{equation}
This identity counts the full neighbor set once. Independence is in the kernel, not necessarily in the image rows; zero image profiles contribute zero. These exact weighted identities are essential.

\subsubsection{Proof by transposed compression}
\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:rs:thm:rooted}]
For $q=2$, use the rank-two-block marginal theorem directly after transposition, with the $a$ original $A$ elements as ordinary columns. Extracting both $B$ rows gives degree $a$.

For $q\ge3$, transpose the graph and force the right set $F$, of size $h=q-2$. Its neighbor set $N$ lies in the original $B$, so
$h\le|N|\le h+2$. Apply the exact compression above. The residual matrix has rank $a+2$.

In the transposed residual matrix, the original $A$ elements remain exactly $a$ ordinary columns. All other columns come from $L\setminus F$. Their span originally had rank $q$ and was supported only on $B$. Eliminating the $h$ independent forced directions reduces this exterior-column rank to $q-h=2$. The kernel family spans the residual directions. Thus the rank-two-block theorem makes the residual degree-$(a+2)$ left marginal Lorentzian. Its left variables are the retained or virtual original-right variables.

Selecting all original $B$ now has a uniform description:
\begin{itemize}
 \item If $|N|=h$, exactly two original $B$ rows remain, and both must be selected.
 \item If $|N|=h+1$, one original $B$ row and one union-profile virtual row must be selected.
 \item If $|N|=h+2=q$, an independent pair from the kernel-line family must be selected. Equation~\eqref{mrn:s:rs:eq:kernelweights} recovers the original full-$N$ activity exactly.
\end{itemize}
Call these distinguished original or virtual rows $D$. Give each its assigned positive activity times a common variable $y$, while keeping all $z_R$ separate. Nonnegative linear substitution preserves the Lorentzian property \cite{b:BH}. The $y$-degree is at most two: there are two distinguished elements in the first cases, and the virtual family has rank two in the last.

Apply $\tfrac12\partial_y^2$ and set $y=0$. Derivatives and nonnegative boundary specialization preserve Lorentzianity. The result is the coefficient of $y^2$, homogeneous of degree $a$, and the exact compression identities identify it, up to a positive factor, with $H_F$. The total size shift is $h+2=q$, and the power of $s$ is $(a+2)-2-|J|=a-|J|$.

Finally, $F$ extends to a size-$q$ exterior-left basis. Together with an $a$-matching from $A$ to $R$, this gives a maximum matching containing $F$ and all $B$. The top coefficient is positive; the same left basis gives a positive constant coefficient. Thus the actual residual degree is $a$, as claimed.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Why the roots cannot be omitted}\label{mrn:s:rs:sec:obstruction}
\mnote{This 25-vertex graph is also the example of source~05's last section (Section~\ref{mrn:src:05}), which uses it, without citing source~23, against a quintic Lorentzian route; it is printed once, here.}
We construct a connected graph whose unrooted all-$B$ cubic fails $\ULC_3$, although Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:rs:thm:rooted} applies after any one exterior-left vertex is additionally selected.

Take $A=\{A_1,A_2,A_3\}$ and $B=\{B_1,B_2,B_3\}$. The core edges are
\[
 A_1B_2,\qquad A_2B_1.
\]
There are eight exterior-left vertices, each adjacent to all of $B$. All left activities and the three $B$ activities are one. The exterior-right vertices are:
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c|c}
 \text{neighborhood in }A&\text{population}&\text{activity per vertex}\\\hline
 \{A_1\}&1&15\\
 \{A_2\}&1&15\\
 \{A_1,A_2\}&7&1\\
 \{A_3\}&1&15\\
 \{A_1,A_3\}&1&1/10
 \end{array}
\]
The graph has 25 vertices and 45 edges. Three common exterior-left vertices match all $B$, and the three private exterior-right vertices match all $A$, so its matching rank is six.

Its minimum cover is uniquely $A\cup B$. Omitting any $B$ vertex would require all eight common left neighbors, impossible in a six-vertex cover. Once all $B$ are selected, omitting either $A_1$ or $A_2$ would require all seven common right neighbors. The remaining one-vertex budget must include $A_3$, because its private neighbor and the hybrid neighbor are distinct. The hybrid joins the former $A_3$ star to the other component, so the graph is connected.

\subsubsection{Exact conditional coefficients}
First give the hybrid vertex activity $\varepsilon$. Removing it leaves an isolated $A_3$ star and a quadratic factor. Direct support counting gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:rs:eq:base}
 C_0(t)=(1+15t)(120+3500t+25536t^2).
\end{equation}
Indeed, with $n=8$, $p=\binom82=28$, $q=\binom83=56$, the constant quadratic coefficient is $q+2p+n=120$. Its linear coefficient is
$2(q+p)15+(2q+p)7=3500$, and its quadratic coefficient is
$q(15^2+30\cdot7+\binom72)=25536$.

The hybrid contributes
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:rs:eq:perturb}
 C_\varepsilon(t)=C_0(t)+\varepsilon(204t+5992t^2+44016t^3).
\end{equation}
For the first increment, $2q+3p+n=204$. Pairing the hybrid with the four old right classes contributes
\[
 15(q+p)+15(2q+p)+7(3q+p)+15(q+p)=5992.
\]
The top increment is
$q(2\cdot15^2+3\cdot15\cdot7+\binom72)=44016$.
These are endpoint-support counts, not counts of matching allocations.

At $\varepsilon=1/10$, the conditioned polynomial is $t^3C(t)$, with
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:rs:eq:counter}
 C(t)=120+\frac{26602}{5}t+\frac{393176}{5}t^2
                    +\frac{1937208}{5}t^3.
\end{equation}
Multiply by the harmless positive factor five, and write
\[
 \widehat C(t)=600+26602t+393176t^2+1937208t^3.
\]
Its degree-three Newton gaps are
\begin{align*}
 26602^2-3\cdot600\cdot393176&=-50396,\\
 393176^2-3\cdot26602\cdot1937208&=-13454672.
\end{align*}
Both fail strictly. Its cubic discriminant is $-9199780352$, so scalar real-rootedness fails as well.

By contrast, the two nontrivial shifted degree-six gaps are
\begin{align*}
 8\cdot26602^2-15\cdot600\cdot393176&=2122747232>0,\\
 5\cdot393176^2-12\cdot26602\cdot1937208&=154533548288>0.
\end{align*}
Thus this is not a counterexample to the required rank-six normalization.
\mnote{The tempting false reading: this cubic does not contradict source~21's all-core bounds (Part~IV). They are bounds for the \emph{shifted} degree-six inequalities, with constants $2n/(n-1)$ and $21/8$, both below three; on this graph the two ratios are $2.99979\ge16/7$ and $2.99974\ge21/8$ (recomputed at placement). Only the \emph{unshifted} degree-three inequalities, with constant three, fail.}

\subsubsection{The recombination obstruction}
Forcing a specified common exterior-left vertex replaces each factor
$\binom8j$ counting selected common left vertices by $\binom7{j-1}$.
Summing over the eight possible roots therefore counts a support $j$ times when it uses $j$ exterior-left vertices:
\[
 8\binom7{j-1}=j\binom8j.
\]
All eight rooted marginals are Lorentzian by Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:rs:thm:rooted}; they do not recover the unrooted marginal with its required multiplicity one. This example shows that the normalization loss cannot be repaired by simply dropping the roots.

\subsection{The original graph is safe under every common core field}
There is a stronger check for this particular graph. Keep the exterior-right activities as above, and give every $B$ vertex a common activity $w>0$. Direct endpoint enumeration gives the full support coefficients
\[
\begin{array}{c|l}
k&p_k(w)\\\hline
0&1\\
1&296/5+26w\\
2&11241/10+(14951/10)w+117w^2\\
3&34593/5+(276443/10)w+(61207/10)w^2+120w^3\\
4&(830232/5)w+(521166/5)w^2+(26602/5)w^3\\
5&(2905812/5)w^2+(393176/5)w^3\\
6&(1937208/5)w^3
\end{array}
\]
Put
$G_k=k(6-k)p_k^2-(k+1)(7-k)p_{k-1}p_{k+1}$.
The third and fourth gaps have nonnegative coefficients and are nonzero. The remaining gaps have the exact forms
\begin{align*}
 5G_1={}&20170-12746w+9880w^2,\\
 25G_2={}&99127242-8903046w+94261217w^2
                    +7629855w^3+1567800w^4,\\
 25G_5={}&w^4(22918732029648-690327945216w
                                  +154533548288w^2).
\end{align*}
The displayed quadratic parts have positive leading coefficients and respective discriminants
\[
 -634657884,\qquad
 -37296153647015940,\qquad
 -13690299259267221125283840.
\]
They are positive for every real $w$. Hence every $G_k(w)>0$ for $w>0$.
The original graph is strictly $\ULC_6$ throughout this common-core-field family, while its all-$B$ cubic fails $\ULC_3$. No statement for arbitrary independent $B$ fields is inferred from this check.

\subsection{Verification and remaining questions}
The package \mnote{shipped as \path{code/23-rooted-sectors-*.py} and \path{data/23-rooted-sectors-*.json}} includes a Hall-subset enumeration of the conditional coefficients, all four hybrid increments, the full common-field coefficient table and its exact gap certificates. A separate endpoint-state dynamic enumeration verifies the original 24-vertex construction. An exhaustive enumeration of all 2048 left-cover masks confirms the connected graph's unique minimum cover and rank.

The Lorentzian theorem depends on the exact weighted matrix/kernel compression inputs \cite{b:m60}. The residual object must not be replaced by its entry-support graph, since its linear dependencies matter. The counterexample uses unit left activities, positive right activities, a genuine $3+3$ cover and no degenerating zero field.

The general finite-activity rank-six one-shore ULC problem remains open. The present obstruction also does not refute an all-core eventual-scaling theorem at the weaker rank-six normalization. \mnote{Such a theorem is source~21's Corollary (Part~IV). On the open problem: at matching number six with one weighted shore, gap one holds by \prp's Theorem~180.2, gap two by source~05 (Section~\ref{mrn:src:05}) and gap five by source~13 (Section~\ref{mrn:src:13}), at every activity; gaps three and four are open in general.} It identifies why the blanket stronger scalar-$\ULC_3$ and real-rootedness routes cannot prove that result. No global minimal-vertex or priority claim is made.

%% ======================== source 13 ========================
\section{The last Newton inequality at matching number six, one weighted shore (source~13)}\label{mrn:src:13}
\paragraph{Abstract of source~13, as delivered.} For every bipartite graph of matching rank six, with unit activities on one shore and arbitrary positive activities on the other, we prove the last matching-rank-normalized Newton inequality. The three core activities reduce the gap to a positive-definite quadratic in their reciprocals. Two matroid Hessians and a weighted plane-incidence inequality reduce its Schur complement to a quantitative two-point Rayleigh bound. In the graph-generic setting, that bound has ten support profiles; exact moment aggregation and 858 rational weighted-square terms certify all ten. The conclusion holds at every finite positive activity. The full rank-six ULC question remains open at the three middle gaps.

\subsection{Statement and support convention}
For a bipartite graph $G=(X,Y)$, define
\[
 P_G(t)=\sum_{\substack{I\subseteq X,\ J\subseteq Y\\
 |I|=|J|,\ G[I,J]\text{ perfectly matchable}}}u_Iv_Jt^{|I|}
       =\sum_kp_kt^k.
\]
Each feasible pair of endpoint sets is counted once, regardless of how many perfect matchings realize it. Its degree is the matching rank.

\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:s:lg:thm:main}
If $G$ has matching rank six, every $u_x=1$, and every $v_y>0$, then
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:last}
 5p_5^2\ge12p_4p_6.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
This is the last $\ULC_6$ inequality. It concerns arbitrary finite positive activities. The first gap is already universally nonnegative by the edge-compatibility Motzkin--Straus argument \cite{b:MS}; the present result leaves only gaps $2,3,4$ as possible failures at rank six. We do not claim full $\ULC_6$. \mnote{The first gap is \prp's Theorem~180.2 (Part~XI), printed with the Motzkin--Straus route in Section~\ref{mrn:w:rf:sec:firstgap}. Gap two is settled by source~05 (Section~\ref{mrn:src:05}); only gaps three and four remain open in general.}

We use the following precise earlier result: if a bipartite graph has a minimum vertex cover with at most two vertices on either shore, its support polynomial is $\ULC_r$ at its actual matching rank $r$, for arbitrary positive activities on both shores. The two-vertex case is \cite[Theorem 1.1]{b:m74}; the pure and singleton cases are proved in Section 2 of that report. \mnote{Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:rf:thm:main} and Section~\ref{mrn:w:rf:sec:lorentz} here; the pure and singleton cases are \prp's Theorem~206.2.} Thus, after choosing a minimum vertex cover, it suffices to treat a genuine $3+3$ cover
\[
 X=A\sqcup L,\quad Y=B\sqcup R,\quad |A|=|B|=3,\quad \nu(G)=6.
\]
There are no $L$--$R$ edges. Each maximum matching avoids core edges, so both exterior blocks have rank three. Isolated vertices may be removed.

We use two established inputs: matroid basis polynomials are Lorentzian, with the usual derivative and nonnegative-specialization closures \cite{b:BH}; and every rank-three matroid is Rayleigh \cite{b:Wagner}. The new finite input is the ten exact identities in Section~\ref{mrn:s:lg:sec:certificate}.

\subsection{The exact reciprocal activity quadratic}
Represent the graph by a matrix whose nonzero edge entries are algebraically independent. A square minor is nonzero exactly when its endpoint sets are matchable: distinct matchings give distinct determinant monomials. Write $K$ for the $3\times3$ core, $\ell_i$ for the exterior-left rows, and $r_j$ for the exterior-right columns. Matrix indeterminates are separate from vertex activities.

Let $q$ count independent triples in $L$, let $p_i$ count pairs matching $B\setminus\{i\}$, and let $n_i$ count left neighbors of $B_i$. All are positive. In the right rank-three matroid on $R$ and the private coordinate basis $E=\{e_1,e_2,e_3\}$, let $U,a,T$ be the weighted basis counts with one, two and three $R$ elements, respectively. Its degree-three Newton inequality gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:rightnewton}
 a^2\ge3TU.
\end{equation}

For each independent left pair $P$, choose a nonzero annihilator column $v_P$ and introduce a virtual right vector $w_P=Kv_P$. Retain every pair label, including zero or parallel virtual vectors. Let $P_B$ count weighted bases consisting of one virtual vector and two $R$ columns, and let $P_V$ count bases with one virtual vector, one $R$ column and one private coordinate vector.

For a left row $\ell$, let $\beta_\ell$ be the total activity of those $r_j$ for which
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:block}
 \begin{pmatrix}K&r_j\\ \ell&0\end{pmatrix}
\end{equation}
is nonsingular, and put $N=\sum_{\ell\in L}\beta_\ell$.
Forcing all $B$ and removing their activity product and $t^3$ gives a residual cubic with
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:C}
 C_1=qU+P_V+N,\qquad C_2=qa+P_B,\qquad C_3=qT.
\end{equation}
The support partition uses three, two or one selected left-exterior vertices. \mnote{The same sector decomposition is source~21's (Part~IV).} Eliminating a selected independent pair gives its virtual column, and complementary private coordinate vectors record the selected $A$ rows once.

For each independent right pair $J$, choose a row normal $b_J$ and introduce a virtual left row $z_J=b_JK$, with activity $v_J$. Let $B_i$ count weighted bases $(\ell,e_i,z_J)$. If exactly $B\setminus\{i\}$ is selected, its residual $t^2$ coefficient is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:E}
 E_i=p_i a+B_i.
\end{equation}
This is the same determinant elimination after transposition. Let $D$ count weighted bases $(\ell,z_J,z_H)$ for distinct pair labels $J,H$. Their activity products may share original $R$ factors.

Write $w_i>0$ for the three $B$ activities, $W=w_1w_2w_3$ and $\tau_i=1/w_i$. Exact endpoint counting gives
\begin{align}
 p_6/W&=qT,\notag\\
 p_5/W&=C_2+T\sum_i p_i\tau_i,\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:top}\\
 p_4/W&=C_1+\sum_iE_i\tau_i+
       T\sum_{i<j}n_k\tau_i\tau_j,\qquad \{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\}.\notag
\end{align}
For example, the top coefficient with exactly two $B$ vertices uses all $A$ and two $L$ vertices, giving $p_iT$. With exactly one $B$ vertex it gives $n_iT$.

Set $x_i=T\tau_i$. The last gap is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:quadratic}
 \frac{5p_5^2-12p_4p_6}{W^2}
       =A_0+2c^{\mathsf T}x+x^{\mathsf T}Qx,
\end{equation}
where
\[
 A_0=5C_2^2-12qTC_1,\qquad
 c_i=5C_2p_i-6qE_i,
\]
and
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:Q}
 Q_{ii}=5p_i^2,\qquad Q_{ij}=5p_ip_j-6qn_k\quad(i\ne j).
\end{equation}

\subsection{Two Schur bounds}
Let $\mathcal N$ have zero diagonal and $\mathcal N_{ij}=n_k/2$. The rank-three left matroid on $L$ and the private coordinates has basis polynomial
\[
 qt^3+\sum_i p_ix_it^2+\sum_{i<j}n_kx_ix_jt+x_1x_2x_3.
\]
Its derivative Hessian, with positive pivot $6q$, gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:H}
 H=pp^{\mathsf T}-3q\mathcal N\succeq0.
\end{equation}
Thus $Q=pp^{\mathsf T}+4H$ is positive definite. Indeed, a vector in its kernel is orthogonal to $p$ and killed by $H$, hence by $\mathcal N$, whereas
\[
 \det\mathcal N=\frac{n_1n_2n_3}{4}>0.
\]
No simplicity, coloop or nonsingular-core assumption is needed.

Adjoin all virtual left rows $z_J$ to this matroid, with a common variable multiplying their activities. The derivative Hessian in the original-left direction gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:aug}
 \begin{pmatrix}
 H&d\\ d^{\mathsf T}&P_B^2-3qD
 \end{pmatrix}\succeq0,
 \qquad d=pP_B-\frac{3q}{2}B.
\end{equation}
To check the factors, before taking its Schur complement the entries at the original-left pivot are $6q$, $2p_i$ and $2P_B$. The private block is $2\mathcal N$, its mixed virtual entries are $B_i$, and the virtual diagonal is $2D$. Eliminate $6q$ and multiply the resulting negative matrix by $-3q/2$.

From \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:E},
\[
 c=(P_B-qa)p+4d.
\]
The Cauchy--Schwarz inequality applied to \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:aug} yields
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:cinverse}
 c^{\mathsf T}Q^{-1}c
 \le(P_B-qa)^2+4P_B^2-12qD.
\end{equation}
For a direct verification, use
$|d^{\mathsf T}y|^2\le(P_B^2-3qD)y^{\mathsf T}Hy$,
then apply the two-term Cauchy--Schwarz inequality to $c^{\mathsf T}y$.
The denominator is $y^{\mathsf T}Qy=(p^{\mathsf T}y)^2+4y^{\mathsf T}Hy$.

Put $\mathcal R=aP_B-TP_V$. Subtraction in \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:cinverse} and \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:C} gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:reduction}
 A_0-c^{\mathsf T}Q^{-1}c
 \ge4q\bigl[q(a^2-3TU)+3(\mathcal R-TN+D)\bigr].
\end{equation}
It remains to establish
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:twobounds}
 D\ge\frac34TN,\qquad \mathcal R\ge\frac14TN.
\end{equation}

\subsection{The weighted plane incidence bound}
We recall the rank-three incidence lemma from the all-core conditional argument \cite{b:m21}, including its short proof. \mnote{Source~21's Lemma (Part~IV); it is the case $b=3$ of source~08s's two-plane incidence lemma (Part~IV). It is printed with its proof here, where it is first used; Part~IV refers back.} For a weighted rank-three configuration and a plane $H_0$, let $\beta$ be the mass outside $H_0$, $q_0$ the weighted basis sum, and $h_\delta$ the pair mass whose span meets $H_0$ in the line $\delta$. Put $h=\sum_\delta h_\delta$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:incidence}
 h^2-\sum_\delta h_\delta^2\ge\frac32q_0\beta.
\end{equation}
Adjoin diffuse generic plane points of total activity $y$ and one point $x_\delta$ on each line. The limiting matroid basis cubic is
\begin{align*}
 q_0t^3+hyt^2+\frac{\beta}{2}y^2t
 +\sum_\delta x_\delta\bigl((h-h_\delta)t^2+\beta yt\bigr)
 +\beta t\sum_{\delta<\eta}x_\delta x_\eta.
\end{align*}
Its $\partial_t$ Hessian has at most one positive eigenvalue. Eliminating its positive $yy$ pivot $\beta$ leaves line block $-\beta I$, cross entries $-2h_\delta$ and $tt$ entry $6q_0-4h^2/\beta$. The remaining Schur complement is nonpositive, which is \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:incidence}. Parallel elements and arbitrary positive weights are allowed.

Apply \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:incidence} to the weighted $R$ configuration for each fixed $\ell$. If $K$ is invertible, the effective plane is $\ker(\ell K^{-1})$. If $\rk K=2$ and $\ell$ is nonzero on $\ker K$, it is $\operatorname{im}K$. In the remaining rank-two cases, and when $\rk K\le1$, $\beta_\ell=0$.

In each nonzero case, independent mapped pairs of right-pair normals correspond exactly to distinct intersection lines with this plane. This follows by restricting the normals to the plane: their projective restrictions agree exactly when their kernels there agree. At rank two the quotient by the kernel of the transpose identifies with the image plane. Consequently the ordered virtual-left pair count at $\ell$ is at least $3T\beta_\ell/2$. The definition of $D$ uses unordered pairs. Summing over the unit left rows proves the first inequality in \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:twobounds}.

\subsection{The quantitative local lemma}
\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:s:lg:lem:local}
Let $E$ be the coordinate basis in dimension three. Let $R$ consist of columns generic independently on specified nonempty coordinate supports, with positive activities. Let $v_1,v_2$ be two independent vectors over the field of the other graph entries, so they are independent of the $R$ indeterminates.

Let $\beta$ be the total $R$ activity outside $\spn(v_1,v_2)$. Let $P$ count weighted bases with two $R$ columns and one of $v_1,v_2$, and let $B$ count bases with one $R$ column, one coordinate element and one of $v_1,v_2$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:local}
 aP-TB\ge\frac12T\beta.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
The graph-generic hypothesis is explicit. The analogous quantitative assertion for arbitrary numerical configurations is not needed and is not proved here.

Write $S_1=\operatorname{supp}(v_1)$, $S_2=\operatorname{supp}(v_2)$ and
$S_0=\operatorname{supp}(v_1\times v_2)$.
A determinant involving one virtual vector and one or two generic $R$ columns is nonzero exactly when the coordinate masks permit a matching. Distinct monomials in the independent $R$ entries cannot cancel. The outside-plane indicator for a column of type $S$ is $\mathbf1_{S\cap S_0\ne\varnothing}$.
Thus the actual normal support is retained; the plane is not replaced by a generic plane.

Up to coordinate permutations and swapping the two virtual vectors, there are ten profiles, listed in Table~\ref{mrn:s:lg:tab:profiles}. We use masks $1,2,4$ for the three coordinates. If $|S_0|=1$, the plane is a coordinate plane and gives the first three types. If $|S_0|=2$, it is spanned by the remaining coordinate axis and a line supported on the other two coordinates, giving four types. If $|S_0|=3$, every nonzero point has support of size at least two; equal size-two supports would give dependent vectors, leaving three types.

Equivalently, on common support coordinates, a zero minor means equality of the ratios $(v_1)_i/(v_2)_i$. Set partitions of these ratios, with the one-product minors forced nonzero, give exactly 55 labeled nonparallel profiles and ten orbits. This is an exhaustive classification, rather than a vector sample.

\subsection{Moments and exact finite identities}\label{mrn:s:lg:sec:certificate}
In one coordinate class, with positive activities $u_j$, set
\[
 s=\sum_j u_j,\qquad y=\sqrt{\sum_j u_j^2},\qquad z=s-y\ge0.
\]
Its elementary symmetric moments satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:moments}
 e_1=y+z,\qquad e_2=yz+\frac{z^2}{2},\qquad
 e_3\le\frac{yz^2}{2}+\frac{z^3}{6}.
\end{equation}
The last inequality follows from $\sum_j u_j^3\le(\sum_j u_j^2)^{3/2}$.
Only $e_1$ matters for a singleton coordinate class, only $e_1,e_2$ for a size-two class, and only the full-support class can contribute $e_3$ to $T$.

Replace the full-support $e_3$ by its upper bound. This lowers
$2aP-T(2B+\beta)$, since its multiplier is nonnegative. The other used moments are exact. Denote the resulting quartic by $F_{S_1,S_2,S_0}$, in the eleven nonnegative variables
\[
 X=(x_1,x_2,x_4,y_3,z_3,y_5,z_5,y_6,z_6,y_7,z_7).
\]
The first three are singleton-class totals.

For complete specification, let $\chi(M_1,\ldots,M_k)$ be the indicator that the masks admit distinct coordinate representatives. For a multiset $I$ of right types, put
$e(I)=\prod_S e_{\operatorname{mult}_I(S)}(S)$, using \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:moments}. Then
\begin{align*}
 a&=\sum_{|I|=2}e(I)\sum_{e\in\{1,2,4\}}\chi(I,e),\\
 P&=\sum_{|I|=2}e(I)\bigl(\chi(I,S_1)+\chi(I,S_2)\bigr),\\
 T&=\sum_{|I|=3}e(I)\chi(I),\\
 B&=\sum_S e_1(S)\sum_{e\in\{1,2,4\}}
          \bigl(\chi(S,e,S_1)+\chi(S,e,S_2)\bigr),\\
 \beta&=\sum_{S\cap S_0\ne\varnothing}e_1(S),
 \qquad F=2aP-T(2B+\beta).
\end{align*}
Multisets and elementary symmetric moments count distinct original column labels exactly once.

For every profile the supplied rational data give the exact identity
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:lg:eq:certificate}
 F=\sum_j\frac{\lambda_jX^{\mu_j}}{2r_j}
                 \bigl(r_jX^{m_j}-X^{n_j}\bigr)^2
       +\sum_\alpha d_\alpha X^\alpha.
\end{equation}
All exponents are nonnegative integers; all $\lambda_j,r_j,d_\alpha$ are positive rationals. Every multiplier $X^{\mu_j}$ is therefore nonnegative on the entire moment domain. There are no residual tolerances in \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:certificate}. This proves $F\ge0$, hence Lemma~\ref{mrn:s:lg:lem:local}.

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering
\caption{The exhaustive virtual profiles and exact certificate sizes.}\label{mrn:s:lg:tab:profiles}
\begin{tabular}{ccc rr}
\toprule
$S_1$&$S_2$&$S_0$&Weighted squares&Positive monomials\\
\midrule
1&2&4&64&716\\
1&3&4&61&718\\
3&3&4&54&728\\
4&3&3&74&839\\
4&7&3&122&792\\
3&7&3&90&821\\
7&7&3&93&798\\
3&5&7&95&807\\
3&7&7&107&807\\
7&7&7&98&775\\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{r}{Total}&858&7801\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

The accompanying standard-library checker independently reconstructs all ten targets from the matching rule and expands every rational term of \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:certificate}. It checks 8680 target monomials. A numerical linear program found the decompositions; exact rational reconstruction and exact coefficient equality are the proof-relevant steps. Two active-basis solutions were solved again over the rationals, and every final residual is exactly zero.

\subsection{Summing the local bound}
For each $\ell$ with $\beta_\ell>0$, choose two other left rows completing it to a basis. The normals of the two pairs containing $\ell$ span $\ker\ell$. Their images under $K$ are independent and span the effective plane whose outside $R$ activity is $\beta_\ell$. Indeed, nonsingularity of \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:block} for some $r_j$ forces the image of $\ker\ell$ to have dimension two; the rank cases were identified above. Apply Lemma~\ref{mrn:s:lg:lem:local} to these two virtual points.

For any individual virtual point $w_P$, the ordinary rank-three Rayleigh theorem gives
\[
 aP_B(w_P)-TP_V(w_P)\ge0.
\]
This follows by summing the Rayleigh inequalities between $w_P$ and the three private coordinate elements, and setting their external activities to zero by continuity. It includes zero and parallel virtual points.

Each labeled left pair can be chosen at most twice, once at each endpoint. Summing the local inequalities and using nonnegativity of every unused individual slack gives
\[
 2(aP_B-TP_V)\ge\frac12T\sum_\ell\beta_\ell.
\]
This is the second bound in \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:twobounds}. Now
\eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:rightnewton}, \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:reduction} and \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:twobounds} imply
$A_0-c^{\mathsf T}Q^{-1}c\ge0$. Finally,
\[
 A_0+2c^{\mathsf T}x+x^{\mathsf T}Qx
 =(x+Q^{-1}c)^{\mathsf T}Q(x+Q^{-1}c)
   +A_0-c^{\mathsf T}Q^{-1}c\ge0.
\]
Equations \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:quadratic} and \eqref{mrn:s:lg:eq:last} prove Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:lg:thm:main}. In fact the reciprocal-core quadratic is nonnegative without restricting $x$ to the positive orthant.

\subsection{Reproduction and remaining scope}
The package \mnote{shipped as \path{code/13-last-newton-*.py} and \path{data/13-last-newton-*.json}} includes the complete ten-profile rational certificate, an independent standard-library replay, the exhaustive ratio-partition orbit checker, and the endpoint-set dynamic program. The latter checks the top-three coefficient formulas, positive definiteness and 1536 full quadratic identities on 512 deterministic graph shapes. These finite graph checks are supplementary; the universal proof is the reduction and exact identities above.

The theorem concerns the last gap at every finite positive one-shore activity. It strengthens the earlier eventual-scaling conclusion in this particular gap. The three middle rank-six inequalities remain open. \mnote{The earlier eventual-scaling conclusion is source~21's (Part~IV). Of the three middle inequalities, the second is settled by source~05; the third and fourth remain open.} The unrestricted-vector quantitative local lemma, optimal constants, equality classification for the new last-gap theorem and global novelty priority are not claimed. These are ordinary mathematical proofs with independently checked exact certificates, not a formal-kernel proof or an externally refereed publication.

%% ======================== source 05 ========================
\section{The second Newton inequality at matching number six, one weighted shore (source~05)}\label{mrn:src:05}
\paragraph{Abstract of source~05, as delivered.} For every bipartite graph of matching rank six, with unit activities on one shore
and arbitrary positive activities on the other, we prove the second
matching-rank-normalized Newton inequality. For a genuine $3+3$ minimum cover,
we prove the stronger Lorentzian property of a degree-three truncation in the
individual right variables. A neighbor-feature Gram identity and exact kernel
compression settle its homogenizing and exterior derivatives. Each remaining
core derivative reduces to a two-dimensional Schur complement. The empty core
column has a direct proof; the other columns have an exact certificate on
$61$ rooted cores and $51$ exterior-basis cones, comprising $50\,037\,694$
nonnegative coefficient checks. The third and fourth rank-six gaps remain open.
An explicit quadratic derivative rules out the analogous universal quintic
Lorentzian approach to the fourth gap.

\subsection{The inequality and its cubic strengthening}
For a bipartite graph $G=(X,Y)$ with positive activities, let
\[
 P_G(t)=\sum_{\substack{I\subseteq X,\ J\subseteq Y\\
 |I|=|J|,\ G[I,J]\text{ perfectly matchable}}}u_Iv_Jt^{|I|}
 =\sum_kp_kt^k.
\]
Every feasible endpoint pair is counted once. We never count its matching
allocations. The degree is the matching rank.

\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:s:sn:thm:main}
If $G$ has matching rank six, $u_x=1$ for every $x\in X$, and $v_y>0$ for every
$y\in Y$, then
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:sn:eq:gap2}
 8p_2^2\ge15p_1p_3.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
This is the second $\ULC_6$ inequality. Together with the universal first gap
and the earlier last-gap theorem, it leaves only gaps $3,4$ open at rank six.
\mnote{The universal first gap is \prp's Theorem~180.2; the earlier last-gap theorem is source~13, Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:lg:thm:main}, which source~05 does not cite.}
It holds at every finite positive activity.

The earlier two-vertex-cover theorem \cite{b:m74} proves all-field actual-rank
ULC whenever a minimum cover has at most two vertices on one shore. We may
therefore assume
\[
 X=A\sqcup L,\quad Y=B\sqcup R,\quad |A|=|B|=3,\quad \nu(G)=6,
\]
with $A\sqcup B$ a vertex cover. Both exterior blocks have rank three, since
every maximum matching avoids core edges.

For a fixed right set $J$, let $a_J$ be the number of left sets that match it.
Put $d_j=a_{\{j\}}$ and
$p_k(\mathbf z)=\sum_{|J|=k}a_Jz_J$.
\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:s:sn:thm:cubic}
For every such genuine $3+3$ cover, the homogeneous polynomial
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:sn:eq:F}
 F(s,\mathbf z)=20s^3+10p_1(\mathbf z)s^2+4p_2(\mathbf z)s+p_3(\mathbf z)
\end{equation}
is Lorentzian.
\end{theorem}
Its support is $M$-convex: it is the homogenized independent-set support of
the rank-three truncation of the right transversal matroid.
Thus it suffices to show that every first derivative has a Hessian with at
most one positive eigenvalue \cite[Theorem 2.25]{b:BH}.
After $z_j=t v_j$, the degree-three Newton inequality gives
$(4p_2)^2\ge3(10p_1)p_3$, which is \eqref{mrn:s:sn:eq:gap2}.

Two precise companion inputs are bundled with this report.
The selected-left marginal of a graph with three left vertices, homogenized
to degree three, is Lorentzian \cite{b:m10}. \mnote{Source~10's Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:tv:thm:marginal}.}
For a displayed $2+q$ cover, the two-vertex lift gives a degree-$(q+2)$
Lorentzian marginal in \emph{all individual variables on the two-core-vertex
shore}, with the opposite-shore activities fixed \cite[Section 3]{b:m74}. \mnote{Source~74's Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:rf:thm:main}, proved in Section~\ref{mrn:w:rf:sec:fulllift} (the retained-variable statement at its end).}
We use this retained-variable statement after transposing a $3+2$ cover.

\subsection{The homogenizing derivative}
We first allow arbitrary positive left activities. Let $d_i$ be the total
activity adjacent to right vertex $i$ and let $a_{ij}$ be its weighted
two-endpoint coefficient. Set
\[
 G_{ii}=d_i^2,\qquad G_{ij}=d_id_j-a_{ij}\quad(i\ne j).
\]
For common-neighborhood mass $s_{ij}$ and squared-activity sum $h_{ij}$,
endpoint counting gives $G_{ij}=(s_{ij}^2+h_{ij})/2$. \mnote{This is source~10's overlap identity~\eqref{mrn:s:tv:eq:overlap}.}

Take one feature for each left vertex, weighted by $u_\ell^2$, and one for
each unordered left pair, weighted by $u_\ell u_m$. A right vertex belongs to
a feature if it meets every left vertex of that feature. Their Gram matrix
$K$ has exactly the off-diagonal entries of $G$, and
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:sn:eq:Gram}
 G=K+\operatorname{diag}\left(
       \sum_{\ell<m:\,\ell,m\sim i}u_\ell u_m\right).
\end{equation}
Split $d=\alpha+n$, according to contributions from $A$ and $L$.
Retain the three singleton $A$ features; by Cauchy their Gram matrix dominates
$\alpha\alpha^{\mathsf T}/3$. Retain all singleton and pair $L$ features and
their diagonal corrections. Applied to the transpose of $B$ versus $L$,
the three-vertex marginal theorem gives the bound $nn^{\mathsf T}/3$.
Every discarded term in \eqref{mrn:s:sn:eq:Gram} is positive semidefinite. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:sn:eq:firstGram}
 G\succeq\frac{\alpha\alpha^{\mathsf T}+nn^{\mathsf T}}3
       \succeq\frac{dd^{\mathsf T}}6.
\end{equation}
The Hessian of $\partial_sF$ has entries $ss=120$, $sz_i=20d_i$ and
$z_iz_j=4a_{ij}$, with zero $z_i^2$ entries. Its positive $s$ pivot leaves the
negative of $4(G-dd^{\mathsf T}/6)$. This settles $\partial_sF$.

\subsection{Exterior derivatives and rank two compression}
The Hessian of $\partial_{z_j}F$ has $s$ pivot $20d_j$, cross entries $4a_{ji}$,
and off-diagonal entries $a_{jik}$. Its required Schur matrix is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:sn:eq:rootQ}
 Q^{(j)}=\frac4{5d_j}\mathbf a_j\mathbf a_j^{\mathsf T}-B^{(j)},
 \qquad (\mathbf a_j)_i=a_{ji},\quad
 B^{(j)}_{ik}=a_{jik}\ (i\ne k),\quad B^{(j)}_{ii}=0.
\end{equation}

\mnote{Lemma~\ref{mrn:s:sn:lem:ranktwo} is source~60's Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:rb:thm:matrix} at $q=0$, transposed; source~05's direct Hessian proof is printed as a second route.}
\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:s:sn:lem:ranktwo}
For any matrix of rank at most two, with arbitrary positive row activities,
its degree-two selected-column regular-minor marginal is Lorentzian.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Ranks zero and one are immediate. Factor a rank-two matrix through a
two-dimensional space and group its nonzero rows by projective direction, with
total activities $w_h$. Put $W=\sum_hw_h$ and $Q=\sum_{h<k}w_hw_k>0$.
Compress parallel column classes by adding their variables, and let $m$ be the
number of distinct nonzero column directions.
A column annihilates either one row direction or none. Its singleton
coefficient is $d_i=W-w_i$, where the nonzero $w_i$ are distinct members of
the row-class list, and other $w_i$ are zero.
Every pair of distinct column directions has coefficient $Q$.
The quadratic Hessian condition is
\[
 QI+\frac12dd^{\mathsf T}-Q\mathbf1\mathbf1^{\mathsf T}\succeq0.
\]
The rank-one Schur criterion reduces this to
$(m-1)(2Q+\sum_i d_i^2)\le(\sum_i d_i)^2$.
The difference is exactly
\[
 \left(W-\sum_iw_i\right)^2+
       (m-1)\left(\sum_hw_h^2-\sum_iw_i^2\right)\ge0.
\]
The support is the degree-two homogenization of a rank-two column matroid's
independent sets. Restoring parallel variables preserves the result.
\end{proof}

Consider a row-block matrix $(U;L)$ in which $U$ has rank at most two and $L$
is graph-generic on three columns $B$, with entries independent over the
field of $U$. Let $\alpha,n$ be the singleton coefficient vectors of the
two blocks, and let $S_U,S_L$ be their weighted common-support Gram matrices.
The mixed two-row determinant is nonzero exactly when one diagonal product
is nonzero, by independence of the $L$ entries. Consequently, with the
definition of $G$ above,
\[
 G=G_U+G_L+S_U\circ S_L+\operatorname{diag}(\alpha_i n_i).
\]
The last two terms are positive semidefinite. Lemma~\ref{mrn:s:sn:lem:ranktwo} and
the three-vertex marginal give
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:sn:eq:mixed}
 G\succeq\frac12\alpha\alpha^{\mathsf T}+\frac13nn^{\mathsf T}
       \succeq\frac15(\alpha+n)(\alpha+n)^{\mathsf T}.
\end{equation}

Now fix $j\in R$. Its neighborhood lies in the three $A$ vertices. Forcing
$j$, dividing by its activity and by $t$, gives exactly $E$ times a residual
regular-minor polynomial, where $E=\sum_{a\sim j}u_a$.
Here is the needed compression explicitly. \mnote{It is the case $h=1$ of source~60's Proposition~\ref{mrn:s:rb:prop:smallslack} and Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:rb:thm:kernel}, including its degree-three example; source~05 does not cite source~60.} One neighbor is deleted. Two
neighbors are replaced by their kernel row of activity $u_1u_2/E$.
Three neighbors are replaced by the three pair-kernel rows, of activities
$u_1u_2/E,u_1u_3/E,u_2u_3/E$. Each single kernel row has the endpoint profile
of its original pair. Every pair has the same three-row minor profile, and
its total activity is
\[
 \frac{u_1u_2u_3(u_1+u_2+u_3)}{E^2}=\frac{u_1u_2u_3}{E}.
\]
Thus all support coefficients have the correct weights, including dependent
images. Kernel rows are obtained by eliminating the forced column; these
identities concern regular minors, not ordinary graph contraction.

The remaining and virtual $A$ rows span a space of dimension at most two.
The unchanged $L$ block is graph-generic on $B$ and independent of them.
Its residual singleton and pair coefficients are $a_{ji}/E$ and $a_{jik}/E$.
Equation~\eqref{mrn:s:sn:eq:mixed}, multiplied by $E=d_j$, is precisely
\eqref{mrn:s:sn:eq:rootQ}. This proves every exterior derivative, even with arbitrary
positive original left activities.

\subsection{Exact core profiles}
We now use unit left activities and force a core column $B_0$. Write
$J,C_1,C_2\subseteq A$ for the core neighborhoods of $B_0,B_1,B_2$.
Let $N_S$ be the exterior-left population of nonempty $B$-mask $S$.
Define
\[
 n=|N_L(B_0)|,\quad m_i=|N_L(B_i)|,\quad
 t_i=|N_L(B_0)\cap N_L(B_i)|,\quad
 p_i=nm_i-\binom{t_i+1}{2},\quad E=n+|J|.
\]
The exterior rank gives $n\ge1$.
Let $h(U,V)$ count two-element $A$ sets matching columns with supports $U,V$,
and let $\chi(U,V,W)$ indicate matchability to all three $A$ vertices.
For every nonempty exterior-right mask $S$, put
\begin{align}
 r_S&=n|S|+h(J,S),\notag\\
 x_i&=p_i+n|C_i|+m_i|J|-t_i|J\cap C_i|+h(J,C_i),\label{mrn:s:sn:eq:profiles}\\
 y_i(S)&=p_i|S|+nh(C_i,S)+m_ih(J,S)\notag\\
 &\quad-t_i\bigl[h(J,S)+h(C_i,S)-h(J\cup C_i,S)\bigr]
             +\chi(J,C_i,S).\notag
\end{align}
These are exactly $a_{B_0R_S},a_{B_0B_i},a_{B_0B_iR_S}$.
For $y_i$, partition by two, one or zero selected $L$ rows.
If the one $L$ row meets both core columns, the remaining $A$ pair uses
either core column with $R_S$. Its Boolean union is $J\cup C_i$; the displayed
subtraction removes the duplicate allocation.

Let $q_{\rm all}$ count bases of all three $B$ columns. To compute it,
add the three core-row mask populations to $N_S$, obtaining $M_S$, and ignore
zero rows. Set $M=\sum_SM_S$ and $d_i'=\sum_{S\ni i}M_S$. Then
\begin{align}
q_{\rm all}={}&\binom M3-\sum_i\binom{M-d_i'}3
   +\sum_i\binom{M_{\{i\}}}3\notag\\
 &-\sum_i\binom{M_{\{i\}}}2
       \sum_{S\supseteq B\setminus\{i\}}M_S.\label{mrn:s:sn:eq:q}
\end{align}
Inclusion-exclusion first excludes a missed column. Every remaining Hall
failure has just one selected row meeting a chosen pair of columns, and the
other two rows meet only the third. The three failure types are disjoint.

The class-limit matrix has seven $R$ coordinates and the two other $B$
coordinates. Its $R$ block and cross columns are
\begin{align}
 Q_R(S,T)&=\frac{4r_Sr_T}{5E}-nh(S,T)-\chi(J,S,T),\label{mrn:s:sn:eq:Rblock}\\
 v_i(S)&=\frac{4x_ir_S}{5E}-y_i(S).\notag
\end{align}
The remaining block has diagonal $4x_i^2/(5E)$ and off-diagonal
$4x_1x_2/(5E)-q_{\rm all}$.
This matrix suffices for all finite class populations: if $X_S$ is the sum
of arbitrary real test coordinates in class $S$, the actual quadratic form
is the class-limit form in $(B_1,B_2,\mathbf X)$ plus
\[
 \sum_S\bigl[nh(S,S)+\chi(J,S,S)\bigr]
           \sum_{r:\,N(r)=S}z_r^2\ge0.
\]
Absent classes set $X_S=0$. This identity does not assume equal right activities.

\subsection{The two dimensional Schur complement}
For $|J|\le1$, all profile functions in a right mask are combinations of the
six containment features $1,2,3,12,13,23$. The mask-$7$ row and column equal
the sum of those for masks $3,5,6$ minus those for masks $1,2,4$, including
the cross terms. We therefore retain masks $1,\ldots,6$.
For $|J|=2,3$ retain all seven.

Every principal matrix containing $R$ and only one other $B_i$ is positive
semidefinite. Indeed, delete the other core vertex. The graph has a genuine
$3+2$ cover. Transpose and apply the full marginal statement of \cite{b:m74}:
its selected-right marginal is Lorentzian of degree five. Differentiate in
$B_0$ and then twice in its homogenizing variable. The quadratic Schur
coefficient is $3/(4E)$, whereas \eqref{mrn:s:sn:eq:rootQ} has $4/(5E)$.
The difference is the positive rank-one matrix
$\mathbf a\mathbf a^{\mathsf T}/(20E)$.
Using arbitrarily many twins with equal test coordinates divided by their
population proves the same assertion for the class-limit principal matrix.

For $J=1,3,7$ in binary-mask notation, direct exact calculation gives the
following determinants for the retained block $A_R=5E Q_R$:
\[
\begin{array}{c|l}
J&\det A_R\\ \hline
1&6250n^4(n+1)^8\\
3&15625n^4(n+2)^7(2n^2+4n+3)\\
7&31250n^3(n+2)^2(n+3)^6(2n^2+7n+9).
\end{array}
\]
They are positive for $n\ge1$. The retained block is therefore positive
definite. After eliminating it, both diagonal entries of the remaining
$2\times2$ matrix are nonnegative by the preceding principal-matrix argument.
Only its determinant remains to be proved nonnegative.

The case $J=\varnothing$ has a direct proof. Let $q$ count $L$ bases, let
$O$ count $L$ pairs matchable both to $\{B_0,B_1\}$ and to $\{B_0,B_2\}$,
and put $e_i=|C_i|$, $c=|C_1\cap C_2|$.
The Schur complement is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:sn:eq:empty}
 D_i=\frac{p_i^2}{2n}+2e_ip_i+n\binom{e_i}{2},\qquad
 L_{12}=\frac{p_1p_2}{2n}-q+cO.
\end{equation}
To verify this identity, use the six containment features above, put
$u=(1,1,1,0,0,0)^{\mathsf T}$ and let $f_i$ be the feature vector of $C_i$.
The $R$ block is $nF^{\mathsf T}(I-uu^{\mathsf T}/5)F$,
the cross vector in feature coordinates is
$nf_i-(p_i+ne_i)u/5$, and the feature inverse is $I+uu^{\mathsf T}/2$.
Substitution cancels the mixed core terms in \eqref{mrn:s:sn:eq:empty}.

Rank-three Rayleigh gives $nq\le p_1p_2$ \cite{b:Wagner}.
Also $O\le\min(p_1,p_2)$ and $c\le\min(e_1,e_2)$.
With $b=p_1p_2/(2n)$ we have $-b\le L_{12}\le b+cO$, while
\[
 D_1D_2-(b+cO)^2\ge
 \frac cn p_1p_2(p_1+p_2-O)+c^2(4p_1p_2-O^2)\ge0.
\]
Thus the empty-column Schur complement is positive semidefinite.

\subsection{The complete finite certificate}\label{mrn:s:sn:sec:certificate}
For the nonempty column, permute $A$ so $J$ is $1,3$ or $7$.
Permutations stabilizing $J$, together with exchange of $B_1,B_2$, leave
$24,24,13$ rooted core orbits respectively. The two shores are never transposed
in this enumeration.

Choose any exterior $L$ basis. Its neighborhood multiset is one of exactly
$51$ Hall-matchable triples among the $84$ triples with repetition from seven
masks. If $b_S$ records this basis, write $N_S=b_S+X_S$ with $X_S\ge0$.
Every genuine exterior block has such a decomposition.

For each core, form the exact rational $2\times2$ Schur complement from
\eqref{mrn:s:sn:eq:profiles}--\eqref{mrn:s:sn:eq:Rblock}. Clear a positive common denominator
and then a positive scalar denominator in its determinant. For every basis
choice, translate $N=b+X$. The complete certificate states that every coefficient
of every resulting ordinary polynomial in the seven $X_S$ is nonnegative.
The exact totals are
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{rrrr}
\toprule
$|J|$&Core orbits&Basis cones&Nonzero coefficient entries\\
\midrule
1&24&1224&$10\,025\,635$\\
2&24&1224&$22\,628\,373$\\
3&13&663&$17\,383\,686$\\
\midrule
Total&61&3111&$50\,037\,694$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Every listed nonzero coefficient is a positive integer; the minimum is one.
Absent monomials have coefficient zero. The cleared determinants have degree
$10$, $12$ or $14$. Their possible common denominators before squaring are
\[
 2n^2,\quad n(2n^2+4n+3),\quad
 2n(2n^2+7n+9),\quad 2n(n+2)(2n^2+7n+9),
\]
all strictly positive on the required domain.

The complete per-core records contain the denominator, scalar multiplier,
degree, coefficient counts and the SHA-256 of each lexicographically ordered
coefficient list. The source reconstructs every coefficient with exact rational
arithmetic and checks its sign. An independent reconstruction uses labeled Hall
tests for the endpoint coefficients, verifies each symbolic $R$ inverse and
uses a different population translation. The full replay status and commands
are recorded in the package's verification document. \mnote{Shipped as \path{05-second-newton-VERIFICATION.md}; the scripts as \path{code/05-second-newton-*.py}, the per-core records as \path{data/05-second-newton-core_schur_*.json} and the independent replays as \path{data/05-second-newton-independent-*.json}.}

These finitely many identities prove every remaining determinant inequality
on every nonnegative real cone $X\ge0$, hence on all original integer
populations. With both diagonal Schur entries nonnegative, each Schur matrix
is positive semidefinite. Sections 2--5 \mnote{Subsections~\ref{mrn:src:05}.2--\ref{mrn:src:05}.5 here} now settle every derivative of
\eqref{mrn:s:sn:eq:F}. Its $M$-convex support completes Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:sn:thm:cubic},
and the minimum-cover reduction proves Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:sn:thm:main}.

\subsection{Scope and the next truncations}
The next possible truncation is
\[
 F_4=15s^4+10p_1s^3+6p_2s^2+3p_3s+p_4,\qquad
 \partial_sF_4=3F.
\]
Its Lorentzian property would prove $9p_3^2\ge16p_2p_4$.
Only the two-distinct-right-variable quadratic derivatives are new.
This is a research direction, not a conclusion of the present theorem.

The analogous quintic
\[
 F_5=6s^5+5p_1s^4+4p_2s^3+3p_3s^2+2p_4s+p_5
\]
is \emph{not} universally Lorentzian. An explicit graph has core row masks
$(2,1,0)$, eight exterior-left vertices of mask $7$, and exterior-right
types $1,2,3,4,5$ with populations $1,1,7,1,1$ and activities
$15,15,1,15,1/10$. All left activities are one. It is connected and has
matching rank six. \mnote{This is source~23's 25-vertex graph (Section~\ref{mrn:s:rs:sec:obstruction}); source~05 does not cite source~23. Its cubic below is source~23's~\eqref{mrn:s:rs:eq:counter}.} Its all-$B$ conditional cubic has coefficients
\[
 (C_0,C_1,C_2,C_3)=
 \left(120,\frac{26602}{5},\frac{393176}{5},\frac{1937208}{5}\right).
\]
Differentiate $F_5$ in the three $B$ variables and specialize the $R$ variables.
The resulting quadratic is $3C_0s^2+2C_1st+C_2t^2$, but
\[
 C_1^2-3C_0C_2=-\frac{50396}{25}.
\]
Its Hessian has positive diagonal and determinant $201584/25$, hence two
positive eigenvalues. The source package directly verifies these counts.
This refutes the stronger quintic approach, not scalar $\ULC_6$.
The fourth gap requires a weaker argument.

The present result is an ordinary mathematical proof with an exact finite
coefficient certificate. It is not proof-assistant formalization or external
refereeing, and no exhaustive priority claim is made.

%% ======================== source 69 ========================
\section{Matching number six with common exterior neighbourhoods, one weighted shore (source~69)}\label{mrn:src:69}
\paragraph{Abstract of source~69, as delivered.} We prove matching-rank ultra-log-concavity for one-shore activities on a rank-six family with an arbitrary $3\times3$ core and complete exterior neighborhoods. The proof reduces the last four Newton gaps to 8,964 explicit univariate coefficient polynomials, all nonnegative coefficientwise; the first gap has a general elementary proof. A small exact verifier and the complete integer certificate accompany the note. We also give a cover-changing reduction that handles certain adjacent exterior classes, and an explicit negative coefficient showing why the main certificate does not extend unchanged to arbitrary exterior neighborhoods. The general rank-six one-shore problem remains unresolved.

\subsection{The theorem and its scope}
For a bipartite graph $G=(X,Y;E)$ with activities $u_x,v_y>0$, put
\[
 P_G(t)=\sum_{(I,J)\,\mathrm{feasible}}u_Iv_Jt^{|I|}
       =\sum_{k=0}^{d}p_kt^k.
\]
Feasibility means $I\subseteq X$, $J\subseteq Y$, $|I|=|J|$, and $G[I,J]$ has a perfect matching. Each endpoint pair is counted once. Thus $P_G$ is the weighted perfectly matchable set polynomial, not the matching polynomial; see Davis--Kohl~\cite{b:DK}. Its degree is $d=\nu(G)$. Define
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ce:eq:gap}
 \gap_k^{(d)}(P)=k(d-k)p_k^2-(k+1)(d-k+1)p_{k-1}p_{k+1}.
\end{equation}
The property $\ULC_d$ means all these gaps are nonnegative, with no internal zero coefficients.

\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:s:ce:thm:main}
Let $X=A\sqcup L$, $Y=B\sqcup R$, where $|A|=|B|=3$, $|L|=n\ge3$ and $|R|=m\ge3$. Let
\[
 E=(L\times B)\cup(A\times R)\cup K,
 \qquad K\subseteq A\times B.
\]
Give every vertex in $X$ activity one and every vertex in $Y$ an arbitrary positive activity. Then $P_G$ has degree six and is $\ULC_6$. The same conclusion holds with the two shores interchanged.
\end{theorem}
The core $K$ may be any of the 512 labeled $3\times3$ bipartite graphs. The exterior-right activities need not agree. A common positive activity on the entire unit-weight shore can also be allowed, by rescaling $t$.

This is a partial rank-six result. Arbitrary positive two-shore fields are known to satisfy rank-normalized ULC through rank five, whereas a one-shore counterexample of rank 38 is available in companion notes~\cite{b:m74,b:m73}. The present theorem excludes this entire common-exterior rank-six family from being a smaller one-shore counterexample. \mnote{Its gaps two and five now also follow from sources~05 and~13 for every one-shore graph of matching number six; what is new here for this family is gaps three and four. Taking $K$ complete and all activities one gives \prp's unit $H_{3,n}$, so the unit-weight assertion of its Corollary~177.2 (\path{hb:cor:asymptoticweight}) is a special case of Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:ce:thm:main}.} It does not settle arbitrary $3+3$ covers or claim exhaustive literature priority. In particular, the ambient-shore normalization of R\"ohrle--Ulirsch~\cite{b:RU} need not equal six here.

\subsection{Exact endpoint counting and the deleted-core baseline}
Write $b_1,b_2,b_3$ for the $B$ activities, $r_1,\ldots,r_m$ for the $R$ activities, and $e_l(R)$ for their elementary symmetric polynomials. For $J\subseteq B$, let $b_J=\prod_{j\in J}b_j$. Out-of-range binomial coefficients are zero.

\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:s:ce:lem:count}
For $0\le k\le6$,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ce:eq:count}
 p_k=\sum_{\substack{J\subseteq B,\ 0\le l\le3\\|J|+l=k}}
 T_{J,l}(n)b_J e_l(R),
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ce:eq:T}
 T_{J,l}(n)=\sum_{\substack{I\subseteq A,\ |I|\ge l\\
 \nu(K[I,J])\ge |I|-l}}
 \binom n{|J|+l-|I|}.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix $I,J$ and $l$ selected exterior-right vertices. A support of this type has $h=|J|+l-|I|$ exterior-left vertices. A perfect matching must use $l$ edges from $I$ to those exterior-right vertices and $h$ edges from the selected exterior-left vertices into $J$. The remaining $|I|-l$ edges are core edges. Hence the stated core matching condition is necessary. Conversely, take that many disjoint core edges, match the remaining $l$ vertices of $I$ to the selected exterior-right vertices, and match the remaining $h$ vertices of $J$ to the selected exterior-left vertices. Completeness of both exterior neighborhoods permits these choices. This proves existence without introducing a multiplicity for different realizing matchings. There are $\binom nh$ possible exterior-left endpoint sets, giving~\eqref{mrn:s:ce:eq:T} and~\eqref{mrn:s:ce:eq:count}.
\end{proof}

Delete all core edges and denote the resulting polynomial by $Q$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ce:eq:Q}
 Q(t)=\left(\sum_{j=0}^3\binom nj e_j(B)t^j\right)
      \left(\sum_{l=0}^3\binom3l e_l(R)t^l\right),
 \qquad T^0_{J,l}(n)=\binom3l\binom n{|J|}.
\end{equation}
Both factors are $\ULC_3$. For the first, the normalized coefficient is
$\binom nj e_j(B)/\binom3j$, a product of two log-concave sequences by Newton's inequalities. For the second, it is $e_l(R)$, again log-concave. Their degree-three homogenizations are Lorentzian; the product is Lorentzian and therefore $Q$ is $\ULC_6$~\cite[Example 2.26 and Corollary 2.32]{b:BH}.

\subsection{The finite nonnegative coefficient certificate}
Set $z=n-3\ge0$. Every right-activity variable occurs with exponent at most two in a Newton gap. A monomial is specified, up to permuting $R$, by its core exponent vector $v\in\{0,1,2\}^3$, the number $\rho$ of exterior variables occurring once, and the number $\tau$ occurring twice. Necessarily
\[
 |v|+\rho+2\tau=2k.
\]
For any coefficient table $T$, define
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ce:eq:C}
 C_{a,b}(T;v,\rho,\tau)=
 \sum_{\substack{J,H\subseteq B\\\mathbf1_J+\mathbf1_H=v}}
 \binom{\rho}{a-|J|-\tau}
 T_{J,a-|J|}(z+3)T_{H,b-|H|}(z+3).
\end{equation}
A summand is zero unless both exterior indices lie in $\{0,1,2,3\}$ and their sum is $\rho+2\tau$. Formula~\eqref{mrn:s:ce:eq:C} is the coefficient of the specified monomial in $p_a p_b$: every squared exterior variable lies in both supports, and the binomial factor chooses the singleton variables belonging to the first support.

Let $D_k(T)=k(6-k)C_{k,k}(T)-(k+1)(7-k)C_{k-1,k+1}(T)$, with the monomial parameters suppressed. The certificate consists of the coefficient vectors in increasing powers of $z$ of
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ce:eq:certificate}
 36\bigl(D_k(T)-D_k(T^0)\bigr),\qquad k=2,3,4,5.
\end{equation}
They are integer vectors because $T$ is a sum of $\binom{z+3}{h}$ for $0\le h\le3$, and
\begin{align*}
 6\binom{z+3}{0}&=6,&6\binom{z+3}{1}&=18+6z,\\
 6\binom{z+3}{2}&=18+15z+3z^2,&
 6\binom{z+3}{3}&=6+11z+6z^2+z^3.
\end{align*}
There are 36 core orbits under separate row and column permutations. The binary mask of $K$ is $\sum_{(i,j)\in K}2^{3i+j}$ with $0\le i,j\le2$. The orbit representatives are
\begin{align*}
 &0,1,3,7,9,10,11,14,15,27,29,31,63,73,74,75,78,79,\\
 &83,84,85,87,91,93,94,95,118,119,127,219,221,223,238,239,255,511.
\end{align*}
The verifier constructs their full orbits and checks that they partition all 512 masks. It then enumerates $v\in\{0,1,2\}^3$, $0\le\tau\le3$, $0\le\rho\le6-\tau$, subject to the displayed total-degree equation, and evaluates~\eqref{mrn:s:ce:eq:certificate} by integer polynomial addition and multiplication.

\begin{lemma}[Exact certificate]\label{mrn:s:ce:lem:cert}
Every integer in every vector~\eqref{mrn:s:ce:eq:certificate} is nonnegative. The complete finite accounting is
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{rrrr}\toprule
Gap $k$&Vectors checked&Nonzero vectors&Nonzero scalar entries\\\midrule
2&1,224&1,001&2,475\\
3&2,196&1,878&5,247\\
4&2,952&1,618&6,105\\
5&2,592&420&2,205\\\midrule
Total&8,964&4,917&16,032\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The file \path{data/exact_certificate.json} lists every vector, including zeros, and \path{checks/verify_certificate.py} independently reconstructs each one from~\eqref{mrn:s:ce:eq:T}--\eqref{mrn:s:ce:eq:certificate} and checks all signs and values.
\end{lemma}
This is a finite exact certificate, not sampling over activity values. For example, at the complete core, gap five and monomial type $(v,\rho,\tau)=((2,2,1),5,0)$, the gap-increment coefficient before multiplication by 36 is
\[
 5(z+2)(z+3)^2(z+6),
\]
which is nonnegative. The complete certificate has the same coefficientwise property, without needing to factor each entry.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:ce:thm:main}]
For $k=2,3,4,5$, Lemma~\ref{mrn:s:ce:lem:cert} gives coefficientwise nonnegativity of $\gap_k^{(6)}(P)-\gap_k^{(6)}(Q)$ in $z$ and all right activities. Since $Q$ is $\ULC_6$, these four gaps of $P$ are nonnegative.

For the first gap, $5p_1^2\ge12p_2$, which is the first gap since $p_0=1$. \mnote{This is \prp's Theorem~180.2 (Part~XI) at $r=6$. Source~69 proves it by the weighted Motzkin--Straus route on the edge-disjointness graph, the route printed in Section~\ref{mrn:w:rf:sec:firstgap}; that argument is not reprinted.}

Finally, $A\sqcup B$ is a six-vertex cover, and disjoint three-matchings $L\to B$ and $A\to R$ exist. Thus the degree is exactly six. Subsets of a maximum matching and positivity of all activities give positive coefficients at every degree from zero through six.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Adjacent exterior classes and a precise barrier}
The following reduction applies to arbitrary exterior neighborhoods and arbitrary positive fields on both shores.

\begin{proposition}\label{mrn:s:ce:prop:compression}
Suppose a graph of matching rank six has a displayed cover $A\sqcup B$ with $|A|=|B|=3$. If some $a\in A$ has only private leaves as its exterior neighbors, then its support polynomial is $\ULC_6$. The analogous statement holds on the other shore.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
All those exterior neighbors have neighborhood exactly $\{a\}$. A feasible support uses at most one of them, so replace them by one leaf whose activity is their sum. This preserves every support coefficient and the matching rank. \mnote{Weighted leaf compression, \prp's Lemma~206.1.} There is at least one such leaf, since a size-six matching forces all of $A$ to match to exterior-right vertices. In the compressed graph, replace $a$ in the cover by its single leaf. The resulting six-vertex minimum cover has split $2+4$. The rank-normalized theorem for a minimum cover meeting a shore in at most two vertices~\cite{b:m74} gives the result. \mnote{Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:rf:thm:main}.}
\end{proof}
For example, this includes arbitrary cores and arbitrary exterior-left neighborhoods when exterior-right neighborhoods are singletons together with the one possible pair class $\{A_1,A_2\}$. Each exterior block has matching rank and minimum-cover size three. If the exterior block between $A$ and $R$ has a minimum cover using at most two vertices of $A$, combining it with $B$ directly gives the same reduction. Thus a genuinely unhandled case must survive both this cover test and private-leaf compression on each shore.

The coefficientwise proof of the main theorem cannot simply be applied after allowing arbitrary exterior neighborhoods. Take complete core $K_{3,3}$ and $n$ unit exterior-left vertices adjacent to all of $B$. Include exterior-right vertices $r_1,r_2,r_3$ with respective neighborhoods $\{A_1\},\{A_2\},\{A_3\}$ and variable activities. If $Q$ again deletes the core, then
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ce:eq:barrier}
 [b_2b_3r_1r_2]\bigl(\gap_2^{(6)}(P)-\gap_2^{(6)}(Q)\bigr)=56-4n.
\end{equation}
This is negative for $n\ge15$. The verifier \path{verify_barrier.py} derives this identity exactly by enumerating left endpoint sets for the relevant right subsets. Yet this graph is $\ULC_6$: its right shore has six vertices, or one can use Proposition~\ref{mrn:s:ce:prop:compression}. Thus~\eqref{mrn:s:ce:eq:barrier} obstructs a proof method, not ULC itself.

\subsection{Reproducibility and remaining question}
Run \texttt{python3 checks/run\_all.py} \mnote{shipped as \path{code/69-common-exterior-run_all.py}; the files \path{data/exact_certificate.json}, \path{checks/verify_certificate.py} and \path{verify_barrier.py} named above are shipped as \path{data/69-common-exterior-exact_certificate.json}, \path{code/69-common-exterior-verify_certificate.py} and \path{code/69-common-exterior-verify_barrier.py}} to regenerate the endpoint checks, compare the complete coefficient certificate, and verify~\eqref{mrn:s:ce:eq:barrier}. The core certificate verifier uses only Python's standard library, integer arithmetic and Hall's subset criterion. It also compares the support criterion with a separate injection-based core matching computation on 100,352 support types across all 512 labeled cores. The mathematical formula and the certificate were independently reconstructed before packaging.

The next unresolved step is a uniform treatment of genuine $3+3$ minimum-cover graphs whose exterior blocks have arbitrary neighborhood classes and survive the cover-changing reductions above. The finite positivity certificate here does not resolve that step. Neither numerical minimization nor a negative coefficient of a gap increment would by itself settle the full one-shore question.

%% ======================== source 63 ========================
\section{Nested families and conditional barriers at matching number six, one weighted shore (source~63)}\label{mrn:src:63}
\paragraph{Abstract of source~63, as delivered.} We extend the rank-six one-shore ULC result to a complete core with nested exterior neighborhoods. An exact finite certificate proves the result for arbitrary positive activities on the weighted shore. We then give a 20-vertex graph with a unique $3+3$ minimum cover whose full right marginal is not Lorentzian, ruling out that stronger approach to the general problem. A conditional theorem excludes every quadratic sector obtained by forcing four right vertices in a genuine $3+3$ cover. The general rank-six one-shore ULC question remains open.

\subsection{A nested exterior theorem}
For a positively weighted bipartite graph $G=(X,Y)$, let
\[
 P_G(t)=\sum_{(I,J)\,\mathrm{feasible}}u_Iv_Jt^{|I|}
       =\sum_{k=0}^{d}p_kt^k,
\]
where a feasible pair has $|I|=|J|$ and $G[I,J]$ admits a perfect matching. Each endpoint pair is counted once. Its degree is the matching rank $d$. We use
\[
 \gap_k^{(d)}(P)=k(d-k)p_k^2-(k+1)(d-k+1)p_{k-1}p_{k+1}
\]
for the rank-normalized Newton gap. Positive support polynomials have no internal zeros, so $\ULC_d$ is equivalent to nonnegativity of these gaps.

\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:s:ne:thm:nested}
Let $A=\{A_1,A_2,A_3\}$ and $B=\{B_1,B_2,B_3\}$. Take the complete core $A\times B$, together with $n\ge3$ exterior-left vertices $L$, each adjacent to all of $B$. Let every exterior-right vertex in $R$ have neighborhood in the chain
\[
 \{A_1\}\subset\{A_1,A_2\}\subset A.
\]
Assume $A$ can be matched into $R$. Give all vertices of $A\cup L$ activity one and the vertices of $B\cup R$ arbitrary positive activities. Then $P_G$ is $\ULC_6$.
\end{theorem}
If the three exterior class sizes are $m_1,m_2,m_3$, the rank assumption is exactly $m_3\ge1$, $m_2+m_3\ge2$, and $m_1+m_2+m_3\ge3$. The complete-core hypothesis is part of this theorem. The earlier common-exterior result allowed an arbitrary core, but that larger core scope is not claimed here. \mnote{The earlier result is source~69 \cite{b:m69}, Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:ce:thm:main}; neither family contains the other. As for source~69, gaps two and five of Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:ne:thm:nested} also follow from sources~05 and~13, and its new content is gaps three and four for this family.}

For $I\subseteq A$ and a selected exterior multiset with class counts $c=(c_1,c_2,c_3)$, define $H(c,I)$ by
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ne:eq:hall}
 c_1\le |I\cap\{A_1\}|,\quad
 c_1+c_2\le |I\cap\{A_1,A_2\}|,\quad
 |c|\le |I|.
\end{equation}
These are precisely the nested Hall conditions for matching those right vertices into $I$. Fix a subset $J\subseteq B$ of size $j$ and an actual set of exterior-right vertices with counts $c$. Its coefficient as a monomial in the individual right activities is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ne:eq:Tnested}
 T_{j,c}(n)=\sum_{\substack{I\subseteq A:\ H(c,I)\\0\le j+|c|-|I|\le j}}
 \binom n{j+|c|-|I|}.
\end{equation}
Indeed, the selected exterior-left set has size $j+|c|-|I|$. Once the selected exterior-right vertices are matched, completeness of the core and of $L\times B$ completes the support exactly when the displayed bounds hold. This counts endpoint sets rather than their realizing matchings.

Delete the core edges and call the resulting polynomial $Q$. Its coefficient $T^0_{j,c}$ is obtained by keeping only $|I|=|c|$ in~\eqref{mrn:s:ne:eq:Tnested}. The graph for $Q$ is a disjoint union of two graphs with shores of size three. The standard transversal-matroid basis specialization makes both factors $\ULC_3$; their degree-three Lorentzian homogenizations have a Lorentzian product. Thus $Q$ is $\ULC_6$~\cite{b:BH,b:m74}.

\begin{lemma}[Finite coefficient certificate]\label{mrn:s:ne:lem:cert}
For $k=2,3,4,5$, every coefficient of $\gap_k^{(6)}(P)-\gap_k^{(6)}(Q)$, expanded in the individual right activities and in $z=n-3$, is nonnegative.
\end{lemma}
Here is the complete finite reduction used by the certificate. A right-activity monomial has core exponent vector $v\in\{0,1,2\}^3$. For each of the three exterior classes let $o_i$ count distinct variables occurring once and $d_i$ count variables occurring twice. Thus
\[
 |v|+|o|+2|d|=2k,\qquad |d|\le3,\qquad |o|+2|d|\le6.
\]
Put $a=\#\{i:v_i=2\}$ and $b=\#\{i:v_i=1\}$. Distribute the singly occurring exterior variables by a vector $e$ with $0\le e_i\le o_i$, and the $b$ core singleton variables by an integer $h\in[0,b]$. The two factors have exterior counts $d+e,d+o-e$ and core counts $a+h,a+b-h$, with multiplicity
\[
 \binom bh\prod_{i=1}^3\binom{o_i}{e_i}.
\]
Use~\eqref{mrn:s:ne:eq:Tnested} for the two support coefficients, and retain the distributions of first-factor degree $k$ with multiplier $k(6-k)$ and those of degree $k-1$ with multiplier $-(k+1)(7-k)$. Subtract the corresponding $T^0$ product. This gives the coefficient in Lemma~\ref{mrn:s:ne:lem:cert} with no activity specialization.

The file \path{data/exact_certificate.json} contains all 3,391 coefficient vectors, scaled by 36 and listed in ascending powers of $z$. They are integer vectors because each $T$ is a sum of $\binom{z+3}{h}$ with $h\le3$. Every entry is nonnegative; the numbers of nonzero vectors at gaps $2,3,4,5$ are respectively $132,495,532,152$. The standard-library verifier reconstructs all vectors from~\eqref{mrn:s:ne:eq:hall}--\eqref{mrn:s:ne:eq:Tnested}, compares them to the stored certificate, and checks their signs. An independent literal matching enumeration checks 80 small weighted graphs.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:ne:thm:nested}]
Lemma~\ref{mrn:s:ne:lem:cert} and $\ULC_6$ of $Q$ prove gaps two through five. The first gap holds for every positive weighted bipartite support polynomial; here it gives $5p_1^2\ge12p_2$. \mnote{This is \prp's Theorem~180.2; source~63's one-sentence Motzkin--Straus justification (edge-disjointness graph, clique number the matching rank, two-matchings at least size-two supports) is the route printed in Section~\ref{mrn:w:rf:sec:firstgap}.}

The cover $A\cup B$ has size six. The assumed three-matching $A\to R$ and a disjoint three-matching $L\to B$ attain that size. The degree is therefore six, as required.
\end{proof}

\subsection{An obstruction to the full marginal approach}
With unit activities on $X$, define the homogeneous right marginal of degree six by
\[
 F_G(s,z)=\sum_{\substack{J\subseteq Y\\|J|\le6}}
 \#\{I\subseteq X:(I,J)\text{ feasible}\}\;z_Js^{6-|J|}.
\]
If $F_G$ were Lorentzian, all positive one-shore specializations would be $\ULC_6$. The converse is not asserted, and the stronger property already fails in the irreducible $3+3$ class.

\begin{proposition}\label{mrn:s:ne:prop:barrier}
There is a 20-vertex graph of matching rank six with a unique minimum vertex cover, of split $3+3$, for which $F_G$ is not Lorentzian.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Use three-bit masks $1,2,4$ for the first, second and third core vertices. The left-core neighborhoods in $B$ are $(2,0,5)$. The seven exterior-left neighborhoods in $B$ are
\[
 (3,6,5,4,3,1,4),
\]
and the seven exterior-right neighborhoods in $A$ are
\[
 (2,5,5,5,1,4,7).
\]
There are no other edges. Each exterior block satisfies $|N(S)|>|S|$ for every nonempty subset of its three core vertices, so it has a three-matching. Also, omitting any nonempty set of core vertices from a cover requires more exterior vertices than were omitted. Thus $A\cup B$ is the unique minimum cover, and the matching rank is six.

Differentiate $F_G$ in all three $B$ variables and the variable of the first exterior-right vertex, whose neighborhood is $\{A_2\}$. This forces $A_2$ into the support. Of the six remaining right variables, four are then common neighbors of $A_1,A_3$, and two are private neighbors, one at each. Their support coefficients are exactly
\[
 \text{constant }64,\quad
 \text{each common singleton }74,\quad
 \text{each private singleton }42,\quad
 \text{each pair }27.
\]
These integers can be obtained by enumerating left subsets of sizes four, five and six and applying Hall's criterion. The accompanying verifier independently obtains them by dynamic enumeration of matchable left endpoint sets; it also enumerates all size-six covers.

Set the four common variables equal to $x$ and both private variables equal to $y$. The resulting quadratic is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ne:eq:badquad}
 64s^2+(296x+84y)s+27(6x^2+8xy+y^2).
\end{equation}
At $x=t,y=19t$, this becomes
\[
 64s^2+1892st+14013t^2,
 \qquad 1892^2-4\cdot64\cdot14013=-7664<0.
\]
A nonnegative bivariate quadratic is Lorentzian exactly when its middle coefficient squared is at least four times the product of its endpoint coefficients. Derivatives and nonnegative linear substitutions preserve Lorentzian polynomials~\cite{b:BH}. Hence $F_G$ cannot be Lorentzian.
\end{proof}
This is not a scalar $\ULC_6$ counterexample. A quadratic sector forced to degrees $4,5,6$ needs the weaker ratio $12/5$, whereas the displayed sector's ratio is close to four. The result specifically rules out universal full-marginal Lorentzianity as a proof route.

%% --- inlined from source 63 file conditioning.tex ---
\subsection{Every four right vertex sector is safe}
\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:s:ne:thm:conditional}
Let $G$ have matching rank six and a displayed minimum cover $A\cup B$ with $|A|=|B|=3$. Give all left vertices activity one and all right vertices arbitrary positive activities.
