Force four designated right vertices by scaling their activities together and taking the leading coefficient of degree four in the scaling parameter. Suppose the resulting sector is
\[
 t^4(c_0+c_1t+c_2t^2),\qquad c_0c_2>0.
\]
Divide out the fixed positive product of the four forced right activities. Then $c_1^2\ge3c_0c_2$. Thus its last rank-six Newton gap is strictly positive, since the required constant is $12/5$.
\end{theorem}
The theorem concerns these conditioned sectors. It does not assert ULC at arbitrary finite activities, and it does not address cubic sectors obtained by forcing only three right vertices.

\subsubsection{Hall tight subfamilies}
If the forced set contains $S$ with $T=N(S)$, $|T|=|S|=h\ge1$, and a perfect matching between $T$ and $S$, every support containing $S$ contains all of $T$, matched to $S$. Deleting both endpoint sets gives a coefficient-preserving correspondence apart from the fixed factor $u_Tv_St^h$.

The remaining graph has rank $6-h$. Indeed, replacing all edges incident to $T$ in a maximum matching by a matching from $T$ to $S$ cannot decrease size. The weighted rank-at-most-five theorem applies to the remaining graph~\cite{b:m74}. \mnote{Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive}.} Force the other $4-h$ right vertices and take a coefficientwise limit at this fixed normalization. Its last gap gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ne:eq:tightbound}
 c_1^2\ge\frac{2(6-h)}{5-h}c_0c_2\ge\frac52c_0c_2,
 \qquad 1\le h\le4.
\end{equation}
This includes a forced exterior vertex with a singleton neighborhood, two forced exterior vertices with a two-vertex combined neighborhood, and any three matchable exterior-right vertices.

\subsubsection{Two core vertices and two exterior vertices}
This case permits arbitrary positive activities on both shores. Let $J\subseteq B$ be the forced pair, and let $z$ be the missing-$B$ activity. Let $E$ be the sum of the activity products of the $A$-pairs that match the two forced exterior-right vertices, and put $a=\prod_{i=1}^3u_{A_i}$. Let $S$ be the union of the core neighborhoods of the possible unmatched $A$ rows, namely those whose complementary $A$-pair matches the two forced exterior vertices. Define weighted exterior-left counts
\begin{align*}
 b&=\#\{L\text{-pairs matching }J\},&
 c&=\#\{L\text{-triples matching }B\},\\
 V&=\#\{L\text{-singletons completing }J\text{ with row }S\},&
 U&=\#\{L\text{-pairs completing }B\text{ with row }S\}.
\end{align*}
Here each count includes the product of the selected left activities. Let $W$ be the total activity of remaining exterior-right vertices that complete the forced exterior pair to a matching of all of $A$. Partitioning supports by whether they select two or three vertices of $A$ gives
\[
 c_0=Eb+aV,\quad
 c_1=(Ec+aU)z+abW,\quad
 c_2=ac zW.
\]
In the three-$A$ case the common product $a$ makes the virtual union row exact: an endpoint support is counted once even if several unmatched rows work. Eligibility in $W$ depends only on the exterior-right triple.

Take the rank-three transversal matroid on the exterior-left elements, the virtual row $S$, and a private element at the missing-$B$ slot. After the exterior-left activity substitution, its basis polynomial in the last two variables is $c+Ux+by+Vxy$. Every rank-three matroid is Rayleigh~\cite[Theorem 1.1]{b:Wagner}, so $bU\ge cV$. Therefore
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ne:eq:rayleighsector}
 c_1^2-4c_0c_2=[(Ec+aU)z-abW]^2+4a^2zW(bU-cV)\ge0.
\end{equation}
This stronger bound settles the two-core/two-exterior case.

\subsubsection{A useful quadratic inequality}
Suppose remaining right elements have parallel-class activity totals $x_1,\ldots,x_m$ and a total $g$ of other elements, each forming an independent pair with every other distinct element. Put $w=g+\sum x_i$. Suppose their linear costs are $q-\delta_i$ on the parallel classes and $q$ on the other elements, where $\delta_i\ge0$ and $\sum\delta_i\le q$. If a pair of independent right elements has coefficient $T>0$, then
\[
 c_1=qw-\sum_i\delta_ix_i,\qquad
 c_2\le\frac{T}{2}\left(w^2-\sum_ix_i^2\right).
\]
The missing term in the second formula is the nonnegative sum of squared individual activities in the generic group. Set $D=q^2-\sum_i\delta_i^2$. Ordinary Cauchy--Schwarz, or the reverse inequality for the quadratic form $z_0^2-\sum z_i^2$, gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:s:ne:eq:cauchy}
 c_1^2\ge D\left(w^2-\sum_ix_i^2\right)\ge\frac{2D}{T}c_2.
\end{equation}
For a direct check, replace $\sum\delta_ix_i$ by $\|\delta\|\|x\|$. Subtracting $D(w^2-\|x\|^2)$ from $(qw-\|\delta\|\|x\|)^2$ leaves $(q\|x\|-\|\delta\|w)^2$.

\subsubsection{All three core vertices and a degree three exterior vertex}
All left activities are now one. Let the forced exterior vertex have neighborhood $A$. Remove isolated exterior-left vertices and let $N$ be the remaining number. Write $T$ for the number of exterior-left triples matching $B$; $T\ge1$. For each core row $A_i$, let $\mathcal P_i$ be the family of exterior-left pairs completed to $B$ by $A_i$. Put
\[
 W=|\mathcal P_1\cup\mathcal P_2\cup\mathcal P_3|,
 \quad a_i=|\mathcal P_i\setminus(\mathcal P_j\cup\mathcal P_k)|,
 \quad A_0=\sum_i a_i\le W.
\]
Let $V$ count exterior-left singletons completed to $B$ by some two core rows. Exact endpoint counting gives
\[
 C:=c_0=3T+3W-A_0+V.
\]
The three singleton-neighborhood classes of remaining right vertices have costs $2T+W-a_i$. Every right vertex with neighborhood size at least two has cost $q=3T+W$. Any pair completes the forced exterior vertex to a matching of $A$, except two vertices in the same singleton class. Thus~\eqref{mrn:s:ne:eq:cauchy} applies with $\delta_i=T+a_i$, since $\sum\delta_i\le q$.

We show $D\ge(3/2)CT$. The exterior-left rank-three matroid has $N$ nonloops. A fixed basis and one distinct replacement basis for every other element show $T\ge N-2$. Since $V\le N$, we have $V\le T+2$. Expanding and using $A_0\le W$ yields
\begin{align*}
 D-\tfrac32CT
 &=\tfrac32T^2+\tfrac32TW-\tfrac12TA_0
   +W^2-\sum_i a_i^2-\tfrac32TV\\
 &\ge T(W-3)+W^2-\sum_i a_i^2\ge0
 \qquad(W\ge3).
\end{align*}

For the small-$W$ boundary, represent the transversal matroid generically and let $F$ be the span of the three core-row vectors. If $\dim F\le1$, then $V=0$, and
\[
 D-2CT=W^2-\sum_i a_i^2-2TV\ge0.
\]
If $\dim F\ge2$, choose a generic vector $e\in F$, avoiding all $N$ exterior-left directions. Contraction by $e$ gives a rank-two matroid on these $N$ nonloops, with exactly $W$ bases: a pair extends $e$ exactly when at least one core row extends it. Hence $W\ge N-1$. If $W\le2$, necessarily $N=3,T=1,W=2$. The sole exterior-left pair not extending $e$ spans $F$, which is two-dimensional. Exactly one exterior-left vector is outside $F$, so $V=1$. Consequently
\[
 D-\tfrac32CT=7-A_0/2-\sum_i a_i^2\ge7-1-4>0.
\]
Together with~\eqref{mrn:s:ne:eq:cauchy}, this proves $c_1^2\ge3c_0c_2$.

\subsubsection{All three core vertices and a lower degree exterior vertex}
Relabel its neighborhood as $\{A_1,A_2\}$. Let $U$ count exterior-left pairs completed by a virtual row with neighborhood $N(A_1)\cup N(A_2)$, let $P$ count those completed by $A_3$, and let $W$ count those completed by at least one of these two rows. Put
\[
 O=U+P-W,\qquad u=U-O,\qquad p=P-O.
\]
Let $V$ count exterior-left singletons completed by those two rows. With $T$ as before,
\[
 C=c_0=2T+U+2P+V=2T+3W-2u-p+V.
\]
The remaining right vertices have two parallel classes: neighborhoods contained in $\{A_1,A_2\}$, of cost $T+P$, and neighborhood $\{A_3\}$, of cost $2T+U$. All other nonempty neighborhoods have cost $q=3T+W$. Apply~\eqref{mrn:s:ne:eq:cauchy} with $\delta_1=2T+u$, $\delta_2=T+p$. Expansion gives
\[
 D=2CT+(2UP-O^2)-2TV.
\]
The previously proved sharp rank-three two-row inequality is
\[
 4UP-2O^2\ge3TV;
\]
apply it to the virtual union row and $A_3$~\cite{b:m74}. \mnote{Source~74's inequality~\eqref{mrn:w:rf:eq:leftkey} (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:rf:thm:three}).} This inequality allows arbitrary positive exterior-left activities. It gives
\[
 D\ge2CT-TV/2\ge\tfrac32CT,
\]
because $C\ge V$. Again $c_1^2\ge3c_0c_2$ follows.

If the forced exterior vertex has degree one, remove it and its forced left neighbor. Two unit core-left rows remain. With the same two-row counts $T,U,P,V,O$, put $W=U+P-O$, $u=U-O$, and $p=P-O$. This time
\[
 C=T+U+P+V,\quad q=2T+W,\quad
 \delta_1=T+u,\quad\delta_2=T+p.
\]
The exact axis costs are $T+P$ and $T+U$, and the generic cost is $q$. The same expansion again gives
\[
 D=2CT+(2UP-O^2)-2TV\ge\tfrac32CT.
\]
Thus the constant three holds in this case as well.

\begin{proof}[Completion of Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:ne:thm:conditional}]
A size-six matching uses all of $A\cup B$ and no core edge. Four feasible forced right vertices contain one, two or three exterior-right vertices. Three are Hall-tight with $h=3$, giving constant three in~\eqref{mrn:s:ne:eq:tightbound}; two are handled by~\eqref{mrn:s:ne:eq:rayleighsector}; one has degree one, two or three and is covered by the corresponding argument above. These cases exhaust the claim.
\end{proof}
%% --- end of conditioning.tex ---

\paragraph{Verification.}
Run \texttt{python3 checks/run\_all.py}. \mnote{Shipped as \path{code/63-nested-exterior-run_all.py}; \path{data/exact_certificate.json} above is \path{data/63-nested-exterior-exact_certificate.json}.} The package uses only Python's standard library and contains the complete nested certificate, independent endpoint checks, and an exact verification of Proposition~\ref{mrn:s:ne:prop:barrier}, and weighted checks of~\eqref{mrn:s:ne:eq:rayleighsector}. The proof of Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:ne:thm:conditional} is analytic; the finite experiments are not its justification. It contains no numerical searches or internal review reports. These are computer-assisted mathematical proofs, not formal theorem-prover verification or claims of exhaustive priority.

The full commit hashes are \path{1b3960d8acdabd983147b7c609bd9dfb2d67a0a0}
(the batch-70 archive of the manuscript now printed as Part~XII of \prp),
\texttt{a866ff9a2} (the batch-68 archive of the Hall-bottleneck manuscript,
now Part~XI) and \path{4a3b6991d0cc12cc09b884e22728e2128a54c6e3} (the commit
that wrote Part~XI). Source~72 cites source~74 by title, and its shipped
\path{72-strictness-SOURCES.md} records a hash of source~74's delivered PDF;
that file is kept as delivered.

\subsection{What each source contributed}\label{mrn:sec:contrib}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{74} (base): the $2+q$ transversal lift
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:rf:thm:main}), weighted normalization through matching
  number five and the least weighted failure rank six
  (Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive}), the cover obstruction
  (Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:coverobstruction}), the forced-sector and
  scaling refinements, the negative family at every rank $\ge6$, the Ehrhart
  corollary, the generic-line matroid model, the sharp all-core constants
  $2q/(q-1)$ and three, the star-core transfer proofs, a second route to the
  first-gap equality case, and quantitative strictness thresholds.
\item \textbf{72}: equality in any one gap only for balanced star forests,
  and strict $\ULC_r$ otherwise, under the cover hypothesis of source~74
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:se:thm:main}); a weighted Chan--Pak contraction
  criterion (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:se:thm:weighted}); a second route to the
  first-gap equality case.
\item \textbf{76}: real stability of the basis polynomial, hence
  real-rootedness of $p_G$, for every graph with a vertex cover of two
  vertices on each shore (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:sb:thm:main}); a second route to
  weighted rank four; the sharp stability criterion for complete $2\times s$
  mixed cores (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:sb:thm:two-s-boundary}) and its 17-vertex
  boundary example.
\item \textbf{75}: the full Hall classification
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:fh:thm:classification}), with a second route to its
  positive half, strictness, real-rootedness for a singleton core, explicit
  asymmetric failures, and a strictly ultra-log-concave rank-five example
  with nonreal zeros.
\item \textbf{71}: a connected all-unit graph with 6916 vertices and matching
  number 3450 that is not $\ULC_{3450}$ (Theorem~\ref{mrn:u:uw:thm:main}); a
  deficiency-one example and infinitely many such; failure of the two
  Davis--Kohl $h^*$-polynomials.
\item \textbf{73}: one-shore counterexamples of matching number 50 (125
  vertices) and 38; the endpoint quadratic and a construction criterion; the
  weighted pendant-arm identity (which contains source~71's) and a rank-310
  transfer example.
\item \textbf{55}: for one common right-core activity on full Hall graphs, the
  least matching number with a failing last gap is exactly 38, with the two
  shapes and minimal populations (864 vertices).
\item \textbf{47}: in the same family no intermediate gap fails through
  matching number 38, so 38 is the least failure rank of full $\ULC$.
\item \textbf{51}: a rank-49 full Hall graph whose last gap holds for every
  activity while the penultimate gap fails.
\item \textbf{60}: Lorentzian lift for matrices with a rank-two exterior
  column block; exact Hall-slack conditioning (slack zero, one, two); forcing
  $a-2$ exterior-right vertices of a genuine $a+q$ cover leaves a
  $\ULC_{q+2}$ factor; eventual strictness with an explicit threshold.
\item \textbf{10}: the degree-three left marginal of any graph with three left
  vertices is Lorentzian (a 5339-monomial determinant certificate); star cores
  on the three-vertex shore give $\ULC_{q+3}$ for all activities on both
  shores, hence all five rank-six inequalities for both star orientations.
\item \textbf{23}: rooted sector marginals are Lorentzian; the 25-vertex graph
  whose all-$B$ conditional cubic fails both unshifted Newton inequalities
  although the graph is strictly $\ULC_6$ for every common core activity.
\item \textbf{13}: the last rank-six inequality for every one-shore graph and
  every finite activity (858 weighted squares, ten profiles).
\item \textbf{05}: the second rank-six inequality likewise, through a
  Lorentzian cubic truncation (a certificate of 50,037,694 coefficients); the
  quintic route is refuted.
\item \textbf{69}: $\ULC_6$ for every $3\times3$ core with complete exterior
  neighbourhoods (8,964 certificate vectors); a private-leaf reduction; a
  barrier coefficient $56-4n$.
\item \textbf{63}: $\ULC_6$ for the complete core with nested exterior
  neighbourhoods; a 20-vertex graph whose full right marginal is not
  Lorentzian; forcing four right vertices gives $c_1^2\ge3c_0c_2$.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Where the merge chose}\label{mrn:sec:choices}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Placement and base.} The batch's manuscripts continue Parts~XI--XIII
  of \prp{} and answer several of its research questions, but they form a
  self-contained programme of 26 archives; they were placed as a new report in
  \path{log-concavity-and-unimodality}, with source~74 as the base because it
  proves the most general theorem and the others cite it. \prp{} is cited,
  not reprinted, and receives dated pointers to this report.
\item \emph{Source~74's layout.} Its eight section files were inlined into
  \texttt{article.tex} and removed; its appendices are ordinary sections of
  Part~I. Its two short lemmas that restate \prp{} (Lemmas~\ref{mrn:w:rf:lem:small}
  and~\ref{mrn:w:rf:lem:singleton}) keep their statements and receive pointer
  proofs. Table~\ref{mrn:w:tab:shapes} was added from its recorded verifier
  output.
\item \emph{Re-proofs of \prp.} Sources~74 and~72 re-prove Part~XI's
  first-gap inequality and its equality case by the Motzkin--Straus route:
  both proofs are printed, marked as second routes. Source~74's rank-six
  witness is Part~XI's $H_{3,33}$ and its $(4,4)$ shape Part~XI's $H_{4,11}$:
  printed with notes. The leaf compression of sources~72, 74 and~76 is
  Part~XIII's; source~76's inversion identity, gluing lemma,
  expected-determinant lemma and $K_{2,k}$ stability are Part~VI's: proofs
  of those are pointers.
\item \emph{Same theorem in the batch.} Source~76's weighted rank four and
  the positive half of source~75's classification are cases of source~74's
  theorem; both are printed in full as second routes, because their proofs
  (real stability; a comparison polynomial) differ from source~74's and give
  more (real-rootedness, strictness).
\item \emph{Stale sentences.} Statements of sources~75 and~76 that the first
  weighted failure rank is five or six, or that rank five is open, are kept
  with notes: source~74 settles them.
\item \emph{Part~II.} Source~71's pendant lemma is printed once, in source~73's
  weighted form (source~71's statement is kept with a pointer proof).
  Source~47's first-gap proof is a pointer to Part~XI of \prp{} (its
  strictness step is kept); its recall of source~55's endpoint theorem is kept
  with pointers to source~55, which is printed before it. Source~73's
  rank-310 example is kept as a remark; it is one-shore, not unit, weighted.
  Statements of sources~55, 47 and~51 that call rank-six one-shore $\ULC$
  open or that leave earlier gaps open are kept with notes.
\item \emph{Part~III.} Source~60's restatement of Part~XII's rank-three
  matrix is a pointer. The 25-vertex graph is printed once, in source~23;
  source~05's description of it is kept with a note. Source~13's incidence
  lemma, which is also source~21's (Part~IV) and a case of source~08s's, is
  printed with source~13's proof, where it is first used. The first-gap
  arguments of sources~69 and~63 are pointers to \prp's Theorem~180.2.
  Source~05's direct proof of its rank-two lemma is kept as a second route of
  source~60's theorem. Sentences of sources~10, 13, 23, 60, 63 and 69 that
  call three middle gaps, or the whole rank-six one-shore problem, open are
  kept with notes giving the status above.
\end{itemize}
